Tests: `supabase/tests/integrations_imports.test.sql`, `core/references.spec.ts`, `core/mapping.spec.ts`, `api/test/integration-imports.e2e-spec.ts` (machines then NCs linked by the old id, re-import writes nothing), `IntegrationFiles.test.jsx`. **Catalogue vs schema (after IN4, no migration):** `core/columns.ts` (`ENTITY_COLUMNS`) says which column each catalogue field fills and lists **every other column with the reason it is never imported**; **a migration that adds a column to an importable table fails `api/test/integration-catalogue-drift.e2e-spec.ts` until it is classified there** (also: missing columns, unmapped fields, an import function not writing a column, a must-give column not required, an offered value no longer allowed). At runtime `CatalogueService` (`core/catalogue.service.ts`, used by the engine and the files/mapping service) reads the CHECK constraints of those tables once per process (`core/column-rules.ts`: `length(...) <= N`, simple anchored patterns, `= ANY (ARRAY[...])`, numeric bounds) and puts length limits, patterns, allowed values and min/max over the written catalogue (labels, offered fields and required stay written; reference fields keep their own); the catalogue response carries `pattern`, `min`, `max`, `minExclusive`; the mapping page shows the limits. **Controller seats and self-activation** (migration `20260109100000_integrations_controller_seat.sql`, applied live 2026-11-09, live guardrails 0 violations): **every `integrations.*` permission also needs an active Controller seat** (`app.holds_controller_seat`), enforced inside `app.has_permission`, `has_permission_somewhere` and `my_permissions`, so the menu, the API guard, RLS and every integration function follow it (other permissions untouched; an organisation where nobody holds a Controller seat has no one who sees integrations). Handing out roles is unaffected: `app.can_assign_role`, `app.can_manage_membership` and the custom-role `role_permissions_insert` policy compare against `app.my_grantable_permissions` (my permissions plus the `integrations.*` my own organisation-wide roles carry). **Activation:** still `integrations.approve` and a different person from whoever marked it ready, **except an organisation-wide Owner or Admin may activate a connection they prepared** (`app.integration_self_approver`; `app.integration_can_activate(connection)` → connection detail `canActivate`, which drives the Activate button). Settings → Integrations shows a "Controller seat holders" notice to anyone without `integrations.read`. Tests seed seats with `pg_temp.give_controller_seat(org, user)` (`supabase/tests/helpers.sql`) and `giveControllerSeats(admin, org, users)` (`api/test/helpers.ts`).
- **Allowed values for link fields in the import mapping** (migration `20260412100000_integration_reference_values.sql`, applied live 2026-11-19; pushing the API before it only leaves the new list route failing and the preview without link results (the check is wrapped so a missing function never fails a preview)): a mapping row for a **link to another record** (`ref` on the catalogue field: site, department, unit, category, supplier, customer, part, machine, employee, gauge type) now shows a line under it and a list of what it can take. `app.integration_reference_values(org, kind, search, limit, offset)` (`integrations.read`; SECURITY DEFINER because the person mapping may hold integrations rights but not read parts or employees; **only a code, a name, a hint such as a department's site or an employee's job title, active and "duplicate" (a department code used at two sites)**; escaped search, ≤ 200 a page) and `app.integration_reference_check(org, kind, mode, connection, values[])` (the import's own lookup rules: code ignoring case, an ambiguous department, by source id for one connection; ≤ 2,000 values). API `GET /integrations/reference-values?kind&q&limit&offset` (`IntegrationReferencesController`; `{ kind, word, total, data }`) and `references` on **`POST /integrations/templates/preview`**: per mapped link field the check over **up to 1,000 rows of the file** (the sample of an API or database) — rows read, different values, how many have no record or an ambiguous one, up to ten examples with their row counts — and the reason is put on the shown rows' errors too (`referenceProblem` in `core/references.ts`, the import's own words). UI `integrations/ReferenceValues.jsx`: `ReferenceCheckLine` (green "All N different values in M rows match a part in Fortress", red with the first values, **See the list** / **See allowed values** before a column is chosen) and `ReferenceValuesDialog` (the file's unmatched values first with their row counts, a debounced search, "Showing N of M" with Show more, a copy button per code, inactive and used-twice marks); `MappingStep` passes `preview.references` to each row; hook `useReferenceValues`. Tests `supabase/tests/integration_reference_values.test.sql`, `integration-templates.e2e-spec.ts` (link fields while mapping; + sweep query), `ReferenceValues.test.jsx`. **Not built:** checking after the steps of a formula on rows beyond the first 1,000, a one-click "create the missing record", values from the source's own earlier imports in the list.
- **Customer and supplier address and currency** (no plan doc; migration `20260413100000_party_addresses_currency.sql`, applied live 2026-11-19; pushing the API before it breaks the customer and supplier endpoints (they select the new columns) and imports of customers and suppliers): both tables now carry **address line 1, 2 and 3, town or city (`city`), county / state / region (`region`), postcode, country (two capitals) and `currency` (ISO 4217, three capitals)**, all optional, shaped by CHECKs (lines ≤ 120, city and region ≤ 80, postcode ≤ 20) and written under the column-level UPDATE grant like the other descriptive columns (`master_data.manage`; suppliers also by `supplier_quality.manage`). Suppliers already had everything but line 3 and the currency (supplier quality register); customers gained all eight. The currency is the one the organisation trades with them in: **information only, it changes no totals** (those stay in the base currency; a site's currency is still the default for costs). API: the shared party schema (`master-data.schemas.ts` `Party`, `partyFields`; codes are capitalised, empty text becomes null) serves `/customers` and `/suppliers`; `CreateSupplierBody` / `SupplierDetail` of supplier quality carry `addressLine3` and `currency` (`SUPPLIER_COLUMNS`). **Imports:** the supplier and customer catalogue share one address block in `party()` (`catalogue.ts`; new field type **`currency`**, read in `mapping.ts` as three letters, capitalised, "gbp" works), `columns.ts` classifies every column (the drift test fails otherwise) and `app.import_customer` / `app.import_supplier` were replaced (a new record takes every mapped field, an update only the mapped ones, a mapped empty value clears it, currency kept in capitals). UI: **`master-data/AddressFields.jsx`** (the one set of fields: three lines, Town or city, County, state or region, Postcode, a Country combo and a Currency combo from `CURRENCY_OPTIONS`) with `lib/address.js` (`EMPTY_ADDRESS`, `addressBody`) is used by the customers / suppliers dialog (`PartiesPage.jsx`, wider now) and the supplier quality `SupplierDialog`; the supplier profile shows line 3 in the address and a Currency row (`addressLine` in `lib/supplierQuality.js`). Tests `supabase/tests/party_addresses_currency.test.sql`, `master-data.e2e-spec.ts`, `integration-imports.e2e-spec.ts` (customers with an address and a currency), `MasterData.test.jsx`, `SupplierQuality.test.jsx`. **Company email, phone and website** (migration `20260414100000_customer_company_contact.sql`, applied live 2026-11-19; pushing the API before it breaks the customer endpoints (they select the new columns) and customer imports): suppliers already had `email`, `phone` and `website`; customers gained the same three (same limits, same column-level grant, `app.import_customer` replaced). They are **the company's own general line (a switchboard, info@, the web site), not a person**: named people, as many as needed with one primary, are the contacts (`supplier_contacts`, `customer_contacts`, each with their own email and phone). **The forms, profile, registers and import page say so**: labels are *Company email / Company phone / Company website* (`master-data/CompanyContactFields.jsx` is the one set of fields, used by the customers / suppliers dialog and the supplier quality dialog, with the hint "People go under Contacts"; `lib/address.js` `EMPTY_COMPANY_CONTACT`, `companyContactBody`), the import catalogue's labels and descriptions say the same (`party()` in `catalogue.ts` holds them for both kinds), and both registers have Company email / phone / website columns hidden until chosen. Nothing sends email to these addresses. **Registers:** both show a **Currency** column by default and the address columns (lines 1–3, town or city, county / state / region, postcode, country) **hidden until chosen** (`master-data/addressColumns.jsx` for the customers list, `supplierColumns.jsx` + `DEFAULT_HIDDEN` for the supplier register, whose combined "Location" column is gone; the supplier list API rows carry the address fields and sort by `addressLine1|city|region|postcode|country|currency`, and its search and CSV cover them); **`DataList` (every master-data list) now has the column menu** and takes `defaultHidden`. **Not built:** a customer profile page, using the currency as the default on purchase orders or cost lines, an address on the supplier receipt or portal views.
- **Costs in more than one currency** (`docs/cost-currency-plan.md`, decisions CU1–CU12; slices CU-A to CU-D built; migration `20260415100000_cost_currency.sql`, applied live 2026-11-20; pushing the API before it breaks the cost endpoints, the NC register and the NC dashboard (they select the new columns)): **"base" on a cost line is the currency of its NC's site** (`sites.currency`; the organisation currency when the NC has no site), with the **organisation currency beside it** for totals across sites (`org_currency`, `org_rate`, `net_org`, `gross_org`). **Nobody types a rate**: the line's trigger (`app.ncr_cost_amounts`) looks the pair up in `public.exchange_rates` (global cache of published rates; **written only by the API login** through `app.system_store_exchange_rates`; `app.exchange_rate_lookup` trusts a past day's entry once fetched on a later UTC day, a same-day one for six hours, an unpublished pair for a week). `rate_status` = `base` (same currency, rate 1) | `estimated` (published; `rate_date` is the business day it was published for) | `actual` (a person stated it) | `needed` (no rate: `exchange_rate`/`net_base`/`net_org` are NULL, so the line adds nothing to any total and sums skip it). `exchange_rate` left the column-level UPDATE grant and a rate sent on INSERT is overwritten. **`app.override_cost_rate(cost, 'rate'|'amount', value, note)`** (`ncr.update` at the NC's site; `amount` = the converted value of the amount entered, so VAT is never guessed) makes the line `actual`, keeps the published rate in `estimated_rate`, records who and why; an `actual` line's organisation figure is its site figure × the published site→organisation rate. **`app.reset_cost_rate`** drops an override / refreshes. Editing only the amount keeps the rate; changing the **currency or incurred date drops an override**. **Credits:** `cost_sign` (generated, -1 for `supplier_recovery` and `customer_charge`) — every total multiplies **net** by it (CU9, CU10; the NC dashboard used gross before, the workflow `total_cost` fact is now `net_org × cost_sign`, patched into `app.system_workflow_facts` by the migration). A site's currency is locked once lines are stated in it, and an NC with costs cannot move to a site in another currency (QM014 / QM005). **Restate (CU11):** `app.restate_cost_report(org)` (`organisation.manage`, changes nothing) lists sites whose lines are stated in another currency; `app.system_restate_costs(org, site)` (API login only) restates them with published rates and leaves the organisation figures exactly as they were. API `api/src/currency/`: `ExchangeRateGateway` (abstract; **`FrankfurterGateway`** asks `api.frankfurter.dev/v1/{day}?base=&symbols=` — 404 or a missing symbol = not published, 422 = refused, other errors throw and **nothing is cached**; `UnavailableGateway` under `NODE_ENV=test` or `EXCHANGE_RATES=off`, so **no test reaches the network: e2e tests override the provider with a fake**), `ExchangeRateService.settle(org|null)` (fetch what `app.system_cost_rates_needed` lists, store, then `app.system_fill_cost_rates`; best effort, never fails a save; the worker step `exchange-rates` runs it for all organisations), `preview`, `CostCurrencyService` (restate). Routes: `POST /ncr-costs/{id}/rate {kind, value, note?}`, `POST /ncr-costs/{id}/reset-rate`, `GET /exchange-rates/preview?from&to&date`, `GET /cost-currency/restate-report`, `POST /cost-currency/restate {siteId, confirm}` (dry run unless `confirm`). Costs carry `rateStatus`, `rateSource`, `rateDate`, `estimatedRate`, `override*`, `costSign`, `orgCurrency/orgRate/netOrg/grossOrg`; `GET /ncrs/{id}/costs` totals = `{ baseCurrency, orgCurrency, net, gross, netOrg, grossOrg, count, byCurrency[], rates{base,estimated,actual,needed} }`; the register's `totalCost` is the NC's net in its own site's currency (`costCurrency`) and sorts/filters by the organisation-currency value; `GET /ncr-dashboard` takes `siteId` (one site → its currency; none → organisation currency; a person who may read one site is shown it) and returns `currency`, `site`, `sites`, `kpis.unconverted`. Env `EXCHANGE_RATES` (frankfurter | off), `FRANKFURTER_URL`. Tests `supabase/tests/cost_currency.test.sql` (seven deliberate breaks caught), `api/test/cost-currency.e2e-spec.ts`, `exchange-rate.gateway.spec.ts`, `ncr-records.e2e-spec.ts`. **CU-C (screens) built:** the cost dialog shows the published rate (no rate field; `exchangeRate` is gone from the bodies), `RateDialog.jsx` overrides by rate or converted amount, the NC cost tracker shows the net total in the site currency (Estimated / Actual / Mixed), the organisation-currency line, spend by currency and a waiting-for-rate notice, the NC dashboard has a site picker and shows its currency, and Settings → Organisation has `RestateCostsCard`. **CU-D done** (exit report `docs/cost-currency-exit-report.md`: live checks clean, advisors nothing new, and `npm run journey` has a cost currency step: estimate, then a stated converted amount makes the line Actual). Not yet tried against the real Frankfurter service.
- **Legacy calibration records (import)** (migration `20260416100000_legacy_calibration_import.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it breaks only the calibration summaries (they select `is_legacy`) and the new import kind): gauges imported with no history all showed as uncalibrated, so a source's old calibrations can be imported as **legacy records** — the outcome the source gave (pass / adjusted / fail), date, next due, certificate number, calibration supplier, technician as text, notes, **no readings** (they would need every one of the gauge type's checks; a legacy record says what was concluded, not how). New importable kind `calibration` ("Calibration records (legacy)"): catalogue fields `gauge` (new `RefKind` `gauge`, matched by asset number or the source id of an earlier gauge import), `calibratedOn`, `outcome`, `nextDueOn`, `certificateNumber`, `performedBy`, `supplier`, `technicianName`, `notes`; write permission `calibration.manage`; **always add only, never matched** (a second import of the same source record writes nothing; settings fixed like NCs) and "records that disappear" does not apply. `calibrations` gains `is_legacy`, `imported_from` (the connection's external system code) and `imported_technician`: **only `app.import_calibration` can write the mark** (transaction setting `app.calibration_legacy`; trigger `calibrations_legacy_rules` refuses a person's insert with it, clears the import marks on an ordinary record and refuses any later change; the marks are outside the UPDATE grant), and **readings cannot be added to a legacy record** (`QM006`). The table's own rules still apply (not in the future, gauge editable, Fortress reference, next due defaults from the gauge's interval), and the existing deferred trigger makes the gauge's last / next dates and status follow the **latest** calibration, so an older legacy record never takes them back; a legacy *fail* that is the latest quarantines the gauge as any fail does, **but a legacy record raises no `calibration.recorded` event** (history must not start workflows or notices). The shared import functions were rewritten from their live definitions by a checked text patch (`pg_temp.patch` in the migration refuses to finish unless the text is found exactly once), so nothing a later migration changed is lost — **use that technique when adding another importable kind**. Gauge asset numbers also join `app.integration_reference_values`. API: calibration summaries carry `legacy`, `importedFrom`, `importedTechnician`; `columns.ts` classifies every `calibrations` column (drift test). UI: a **Legacy record** pill on the record page and in the gauge's history ("By" shows the imported technician), the record page replaces the Readings card with a notice (what was concluded, who did it, certificates can still be attached), import labels and descriptions in `lib/integrations.js`. Tests `supabase/tests/legacy_calibration_import.test.sql`, `api/test/legacy-calibration-import.e2e-spec.ts`, `Calibration.test.jsx`. **Not built:** importing readings (a second kind, one row per gauge and check), correcting a legacy record's outcome in the screen. **`npm run journey` has a legacy calibration step** (`legacyCalibrations()` in `scripts/journey/journey.cjs`: a CSV of two old calibrations imported through the wizard against a gauge, both marked legacy with no readings, the gauge shows its latest dates and the Legacy record pill, the record page says it has no readings, the dashboard says two are left out, then Settings → System preferences counts them and the dashboard says so; it found that the Gauges tile's description contained "next due", which made the wizard's `Next` button lookup ambiguous: **a tile's words must not contain "Next"**). **Dashboard choice** (migration `20260417100000_legacy_calibrations_in_dashboard.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it breaks the organisation endpoints and the calibration dashboard, which select the new column): `organisation_settings.include_legacy_calibrations` (default **off**, column-level UPDATE like the other organisation settings, `organisation.manage`) — Settings → **System preferences** (`SettingsSystemPreferences.jsx`, which used to be an empty placeholder; optimistic switch) decides whether `GET /calibration-dashboard`'s period figures (calibrated, previous period, failed, first-time pass rate, the results chart) count legacy records; the gauges' own state (in use, overdue, due soon, quarantined, compliance) and the home page's counts always follow the latest calibration, legacy or not. The answer carries `legacy { included, inPeriod }` and the dashboard says how many are left out (or counted) and where to change it. Tests in `legacy-calibration-import.e2e-spec.ts`, `SettingsSystemPreferences.test.jsx`, `Calibration.test.jsx`.
- **Integrations IN5 — production orders, field ownership, review queue** (`docs/connectors-plan.md` §6.12; migration `20260120100000_integration_ownership_production_orders.sql`, applied live 2026-11-10; pushing the API before it breaks the runs endpoints (they read `parked`) and the review endpoints): **production orders** are importable (`app.import_production_order`; catalogue order number, part, part revision letter, customer, site, quantity planned, due date, status, reason cancelled, notes; write permission `production.manage`; matched by order number). A new order keeps the source's status and gets `external_system`/`external_id` (people still cannot set them: the rules trigger honours `app.integration_import` only inside the import); an update moves the status **one allowed step at a time** (`app.production_order_status_path` over `app.production_order_transitions()`, which the rules trigger now uses too), so open operations, closed/cancelled orders and impossible moves fail the row with the reason. **Field ownership is enforced in the database** on updates (`app.system_import_record`, from the run's frozen template version or mapping): `qms_wins` fields (and their `<field>Id`/`Code`) are not written; `manual_review` fields are tried in an always-undone sub-transaction (`app.integration_review_change`) and, when they would change the record, parked in **`integration_conflicts`** (functions only, audited, RLS `integrations.read`, frozen once decided, never deleted; one open per record and field; a newer value supersedes; an agreeing value clears; a rejected or already-waiting value is not parked again; `fortress_change` or `apply_error`); `integration_sync_runs.parked`. `app.decide_integration_conflict(id, accept, note)` (`integrations.run` + `app.integration_may_write`; accept writes as the person under the record's rules; reject needs a reason ≥ 3). "Review" is refused for custom fields and fields that only add (`noReview`, part revision; entities catalogue `reviewable`). API `api/src/integrations/integration-conflicts.*`: `GET /integrations/conflicts` (`status` open|decided|all|…, `entityType`, `connectionId`, `runId`, `recordId`, paging; `openCount`; values in words, links by code, `canDecide`), `POST /integrations/conflicts/{id}/accept {note?}|reject {reason}`; runs carry `counts.parked`. UI: `MappingStep` asks "When the record is already in Fortress" (Source wins / Fortress wins / Review, `ownershipOptions`, only when the import updates and not for NCs; rows keep `ownership`), `integrations/ReviewPage.jsx` at `/Settings/Integrations/Review` (Waiting/Decided/All, `?run=`, Accept / Keep Fortress's with a reason, optimistic via `useDecideConflict`), notice on Settings → Integrations, "To review" figure on the run page, `recordPath` in `lib/integrations.js`. The drift test now finds `app.import_<kind>` by name. Tests: `supabase/tests/integration_ownership.test.sql` (4 mutants caught), `api/test/integration-ownership.e2e-spec.ts` (+ API sweep), `core/mapping.spec.ts`, `IntegrationReview.test.jsx`. **Not built:** reviewing custom fields, bulk decisions, notifications, production operations.
- **Integrations IN6 — purchase orders, goods receipts, received totals** (`docs/connectors-plan.md` §6.13–§6.14, decisions E1–E4; migration `20260121100000_integration_purchasing.sql`, applied live 2026-11-10; pushing the API before it breaks the entities and import endpoints): **purchase orders** (`purchase_order`, one row per order line; key column = order number, catalogue `keySuffix: 'lineNumber'` makes the external id `"<order>/<line>"`; `app.import_purchase_order` finds or makes the order, requested date fixed on create, agreed date/quantity/unit/part/status follow, supplier/order/line number never change, a received line keeps its part, `app.po_follow_lines`). **Goods receipts** (`goods_receipt`, always add-only, never matched; key = transaction id): historical mode — rows with one document per supplier make one receipt (`goods_receipts.source_connection_id/source_document`), real dates, closed orders accepted, lot made released, a determination "no inspection required"; **a return** (negative quantity or movement return) is a rejected decision with that quantity on the latest matching earlier delivery (E1); a delivery is never changed afterwards; field **Leave this row out** (formula-friendly) stages the row as skipped (`skip` honoured by `app.system_stage_integration_records`, engine sets it from `canonical.leaveOut`). **Received quantities** (`po_receipt_total`, always add-and-update): the source's running total per order line is kept in `integration_po_line_totals` (also from a PO import's *Received so far*), a higher total records a delivery of the difference; **received quantities check** `app.integration_receipt_reconciliation` → `GET /integrations/connections/{id}/reconciliation?all=` (short / over / date / match), `ReconciliationCard` on the connection page (needs `receiving.read`). Column map: `FieldColumn.ownRules` (keep the DB rule off a field, e.g. a negative delivery quantity) and `EntityColumns.virtual` (fields with no column; the drift test allows them). `ADD_ONLY_KINDS` / `ADD_UPDATE_KINDS` in `integration-files.service.ts` and `lib/importTemplates.js` fix the write mode. Older e2e tests read the kinds from `CATALOGUE`. Tests: `supabase/tests/integration_purchasing.test.sql` (5 mutants caught), `api/test/integration-purchasing.e2e-spec.ts` (+ both sweeps), `ReceivedQuantities.test.jsx`. **Not built:** reconciliation net of returns, returns in the totals shape, production order operations.
- **Integrations IN7 — the generic REST connector** (`docs/connectors-plan.md` §6.15; migration `20260122100000_integration_rest_connector.sql`, applied live 2026-11-10; pushing the API before it breaks the allowed-hosts endpoints and the connection test, which read `integration_allowed_hosts`): **every outbound call goes through `api/src/integrations/core/safe-http.ts` (`SafeHttpClient`)** — https, port 443, a host name only (no IP literal, `localhost` or internal suffix, no user:password); every resolved address must be public (`isPublicAddress`: loopback, private, CGNAT, link-local incl. metadata, unique-local, mapped/NAT64 forms…); the TLS socket is pinned to the checked address; **redirects are never followed**; timeouts and a 10 MB cap; no `Host`/hop-by-hop/cookie/proxy/forwarding header (`headerProblem`, `FORBIDDEN_HEADERS`). `RestApiAdapter` (`providers/rest-api/`, settings schema `rest-config.ts`): API key header / bearer / basic, paging `none|page|offset|cursor|next_link` (next links same origin only; `header` = Link header), `recordsPath` (dotted, own properties only, `@odata.nextLink` works), fixed `query`, `incremental {param, field}` (saved cursor `{"w": highest}`), records flattened to dotted text columns (`flattenRecord`), 429 → wait for Retry-After, 5xx/408/timeouts retried, 401/403 and other 4xx stop. Connection config keys `testPath`, `entities` checked by `app.integration_config_problem` / `app.integration_rest_entity_problem` (paths below the address: no `//`, `.`/`..`, `%2e`, `?`, `#`). **Allow-list** `organisation_settings.integration_allowed_hosts` (functions only: `app.set_integration_allowed_hosts`, `integrations.manage`; empty = any public host; an entry allows sub-domains; changing it rechecks API connections, pausing active ones it no longer allows; `app.integration_host_allowed`; the API login reads it with `app.system_integration_allowed_hosts` and passes `allowedHosts` to adapters in the engine, the test and the sample). `app.request_integration_run` for REST: the kind of record must be configured, the host allowed, incremental configured for an incremental run; a preview may run without a template (the run dialog asks for one to import). API: `GET|PUT /integrations/allowed-hosts`, `POST /integrations/connections/{id}/sample {entityType}` (first page, nothing stored), `POST /integrations/templates/preview` takes `connectionId` instead of `fileId`. UI: REST card available (**Connect an API** → `ConnectionForm initialProvider`), Records section in the connection form (`RestRecords`; helpers `restEntitiesToRows` / `restRowsToEntities` / `restRecordProblem` / `PAGING_STYLES` in `lib/integrations.js`), **Allowed hosts** card on Settings → Integrations (optimistic `useSetAllowedHosts`), `ApiSampleCard.jsx` ("What the API sends") on an API connection's page (`useApiSample`), run dialog offers only configured records and a template (Full / Incremental need one). **No e2e test may reach the network**: the REST e2e (`api/test/integration-rest.e2e-spec.ts`) injects DNS and the last hop into the real client; `integrations.e2e-spec.ts` stops its test with the allow-list. Tests: `safe-http.spec.ts`, `rest-api.adapter.spec.ts`, `supabase/tests/integration_rest_connector.test.sql`, `integration-rest.e2e-spec.ts` (+ sweep), `RestConnector.test.jsx`. **Not built:** schedules (IN8, since built), OAuth client credentials, POST queries, custom headers, non-JSON answers. **Map fields for an API connection** (after IN7, no migration): `integrations/ApiMappingPage.jsx` at `/Settings/Integrations/:id/map?entity&template` (`apiMappingPath` in `lib/integrations.js`) reads the API's first page once (`useApiSampleQuery`, cached for the session, **Read again** refetches; nothing stored) and feeds its dotted columns and rows to the same `MappingStep` as the file wizard, with the same Template card, `TemplateSettingsCard` and `SaveTemplateDialog` (exported from `CsvImportWizard.jsx`, with `KEY_GUESS`); examples come from `POST /integrations/templates/preview {connectionId}` (the server reads the API again, so the page waits 800 ms after a change); a change is saved as a template version or a new template before Preview / Import; a preview works before activation, importing needs the connection active. Reached from **Map fields** on "What the API sends", the Run dialog's "no template yet" notice and an API preview's run page (Import these records / Change the mapping). Tests `ApiMapping.test.jsx`.
- **Integrations IN8 — schedules, records that disappeared, rows that need a person, health** (`docs/connectors-plan.md` §6.16, decisions F1–F3; migration `20260123100000_integration_schedules_missing.sql`, applied live 2026-11-10, live guardrails 0 violations; pushing the API before it breaks the settings, runs and health endpoints): **daily schedule** per API connection and kind of record with an import template (`integration_import_settings.template_id`, `schedule_set_by` = whoever switched it on, `last_scheduled_at`, `last_schedule_problem`; `app.set_integration_import_settings` takes `templateId`; daily needs an API connection, the records configured, a template for that kind and the person's right to run and write), asked for once a day by `app.system_schedule_integration_runs` (API login only; acts as the scheduler with their stored authority, re-checked daily; incremental when configured and a cursor exists, else full; **never beside a run still waiting or running**, so a failure recovers without duplicates) via cron `GET|POST /internal/cron/integration-schedule` (schedule then work due runs; `api/vercel.json` 04:00 UTC, production only). **Records that disappeared** (`integration_missing_records`, functions only, audited, frozen once decided, never deleted): found inside `app.system_finish_integration_run` after a finished **full** read (`app.integration_detect_missing`; skipped when a row had no identifier; not for NCs, goods receipts, received quantities — `app.integration_entity_tracks_missing`); "list" lists, "make inactive" deactivates when ≤ 20% of the kind's imported records would go, else **held**; `app.decide_missing_records(ids, deactivate, note)` (`integrations.run` + write right; keep needs a reason; a kept record is not asked about again until the source sends it); returned ones are marked; runs carry `missing`. **Rejected/failed rows:** inbox status `ignored` (`ignored_from/at/by`, `ignore_reason`) via `app.ignore_integration_rows(connection, rows, reason)` / `app.restore_integration_rows`; Fortress never edits a source row. **Health:** `app.integration_health(org)` (`integrations.read`). API `integration-monitor.*`: `GET /integrations/health` (+ `state` ok/attention/failing, `counts`), `GET /integrations/missing-records?connectionId&entityType&runId&status=open|decided|all`, `POST /integrations/missing-records/decide {ids, deactivate, note?}`, `GET /integrations/connections/{id}/attention?entityType&ignored=exclude|only`, `POST …/rows/ignore {rowIds, reason}|rows/restore {rowIds}`; settings carry `template`, `scheduledBy`, `lastScheduledAt`, `scheduleProblem`, `tracksMissing`. UI `integrations/KeepHealthy.jsx`: `ScheduleCard` (API connection page: By hand / Daily, template, when a record disappears; optimistic), `MissingCard` (connection and run page: keep with a reason / make inactive; optimistic), `AttentionCard` (rows needing a person / ignored; ignore with a reason, bring back; optimistic), `HealthCard` (Settings → Integrations), import health counts on the master data overview; run page tile "No longer in the source" (`missingSummary`), `MISSING_OPTIONS`, `HEALTH_STATES` in `lib/integrations.js`; hooks `useIntegrationHealth|useMissingRecords|useDecideMissing|useAttentionRows|useIgnoreRows`. Tests: `supabase/tests/integration_schedules_missing.test.sql`, `api/test/integration-schedule.e2e-spec.ts` (+ sweep), `KeepHealthy.test.jsx`, `MasterDataDashboard.test.jsx`. **Not built:** a time per connection, email/push, editing a rejected row, reactivating a returned record.
- **Integrations IN9 — exit check** (`docs/connectors-exit-report.md`, no migration): the browser journey's `importFromApi` step connects a REST API in the browser (address, bearer token, records with paging), tests, marks ready, activates, maps fields from the API's sample, imports, imports again with nothing written, fixes a rejected record in the source and re-imports, ignores another with a reason, and revokes the credential (the test then fails without calling out). The fake ERP lives in `scripts/journey/stack.cjs` (`startFakeErp`, `erpConnectors`: the real `RestApiAdapter`/`SafeHttpClient`, only DNS and the last hop swapped; `stack.erp.machines` is changed between imports). Found and fixed: **an API connection starts from the template its own latest import of that kind used, else a new mapping** (`connectionTemplate` in `lib/importTemplates.js`, used by `ApiMappingPage` and the Run dialog; files keep `defaultTemplate`). `MappingStep` takes `source="file"|"api"` (`SOURCE_WORDS`): an API page says "field the API sends", "Field that identifies each record", combos labelled "API field for <field>"; file pages are unchanged. Phase 5 is complete.
- **Import field locks** (`docs/import-field-locks-plan.md`, decision LK-D1 agreed 2026-11-22; slices LK1–LK4; migration `20260418100000_import_field_locks.sql`, applied live 2026-11-22 (8 guard triggers, helpers not callable by anon, 117 imported records, no schedule on yet so nothing locked; recorded in the live migration list as `20261122124402_import_field_locks`; pushing the API before it leaves only `GET /import-locks` failing)): **while a scheduled import owns a field, Fortress refuses a person's edit of it and the forms show it disabled with a lock and a tooltip.** A field of a record is locked, worked out live and never stored, when: the record has a row in `integration_external_records` for connection C; C is `active`; the (C, kind) settings have `schedule <> 'manual'`; the schedule's template (not archived), at its **current version**, writes with `add_update` or `update_only` (a template always records its write mode and a run takes it over the connection's, so the connection's own write mode does not matter); and the template maps the field with ownership **source wins** (not `qms_wins`, not `manual_review`). So switching the schedule off, pausing or retiring the connection, archiving the template or moving the field to Fortress wins / review unlocks it at once; an offline agent, a failing run or a record missing from the source does **not** unlock; no admin bypass. Eight kinds: supplier, customer, part, machine, employee, gauge_type, gauge, production_order (NCs, receipts, legacy calibrations and received totals are add-only; part revisions and purchase order lines are **not** covered yet). **Database:** `app.integration_lock_table(kind)`, `app.integration_field_columns(kind)` (catalogue field → column, mirrors `ENTITY_COLUMNS`; `integration-catalogue-drift.e2e-spec.ts` compares them and fails when a kind is neither lockable nor listed as not), `app.integration_locked_internal(org, kind, record)` (the rule, once; not callable by people), `app.integration_locked_fields(org, kind, record)` (members who can read that kind; only for records an import manages) and trigger `zz_integration_lock_guard` (BEFORE UPDATE, last by name) on the eight tables: for `authenticated` it refuses a changed locked column with **`QM033`** (unchanged values pass, so a form that sends every field still saves); the import is exempt through `app.source = 'integration'` (set by `app.system_import_record`; SQL run as the system is exempt too). **API:** `GET /import-locks?entityType&recordId` (any member; `{ data: [{ field, connection: { id, name } }] }`, `field` = the catalogue key), `QM033` → `409 field_managed_by_import` with `details { fields, connection }`. **UI:** `ui/Tooltip.jsx`, `ui/LockedMark.jsx` (a lock button inside the label; the input's accessible name reads "Name Managed by ERP"), `hooks/useImportLocks(kind, recordId)` → `lock("name")` = `{ lockedBy, disabled: true }` or `{}` to spread on a control, `lib/importLocks.js` (`lockMessage`, `lockKey` = a form's name → catalogue key such as `siteId` → `site`, `fieldsOwnedBySource`); `lockedBy` is accepted by `Field`, `TextInput`, `RHFComboBox` (so `ComboField` and the pickers), `RHFDatePickerPopout`, `RHFTextArea`, `TextAreaField`, `Switch` and `SelectField`, and `AddressFields` / `CompanyContactFields` take a `lock` function. Wired into the customers and suppliers dialog, the supplier quality dialog, part details, machines, employees, gauge types (Settings), the gauge page and the production order dialog. The mapping page's ownership words say what each choice means for people, and the connection's Schedule card says which fields the schedule locks (`KeepHealthy.jsx`). **A new importable kind that updates records needs:** its table in `app.integration_lock_table`, its map in `app.integration_field_columns`, the guard trigger, its lock calls in the record's form, and its place in `LOCKABLE` (drift test) and `LOCKABLE_KINDS`. Tests `supabase/tests/import_field_locks.test.sql` (eight of nine deliberate breaks caught; the ninth removes a redundant membership check), `api/test/import-locks.e2e-spec.ts`, `ui/LockedFields.test.jsx`, `master-data/MasterData.test.jsx`, `KeepHealthy.test.jsx`, `lib/importLocks.test.js`; the journey (`importFromApi`) switches a schedule on, sees the lock and the tooltip, edits an unlocked field, has the database refuse a direct change and sees the field free again. **Custom fields (`cf_<key>`) lock the same way** (migration `20260419100000_custom_field_locks.sql`, applied live 2026-11-22 through the SQL editor, so it has no row in the migration history list; checked live: the rule, the table guard and `set_custom_values` are the new versions, the two lock functions are not callable by anon or signed-in users, no schedule is on so nothing is locked yet; no API code changed): a template that maps `cf_<key>` as source wins on an updating, scheduled, active connection locks that custom field of the record (the field must be active; retired ones never lock). `app.integration_locked_internal` now also returns `cf_` fields (column `col` is null) and the table guard looks only at rows with a column; people write custom values through **`app.set_custom_values`**, which now refuses a changed locked field with `QM033` (message names the fields by label, the detail carries `cf_<key>` as `GET /import-locks` does; an unchanged value, compared once tidied, passes). The import writes custom values through `app.write_custom_values` (`app.import_custom_values`), which is untouched, so it is never refused; SQL run as the system is exempt. `GET /import-locks` lists `cf_<key>` with the columns, so the More details card (`CustomFieldsCard`, `useImportLocks`, only when editable on one of the eight lockable kinds) disables those controls with the lock mark in the label (`lockedBy` for date and combo fields, `disabled` for text, yes / no and chips) and `fieldsOwnedBySource` lists them, by label, in the Schedule card's note. **An import never clears a custom value** (an empty value is skipped), so a locked custom field the source leaves empty stays as it is: switch the field to Fortress wins or the schedule off to clear it. Tests `supabase/tests/custom_field_locks.test.sql` (two deliberate breaks caught), `import-locks.e2e-spec.ts`, `CustomFieldsCard.test.jsx`, `KeepHealthy.test.jsx`, `lib/importLocks.test.js`; the browser journey (`importFromApi`) covers them: two machine custom fields (Serial number, mapped from the ERP, and Operator note, not mapped) are made in Settings, the import writes the serial number, with the schedule on its control is disabled with the lock mark and the note stays editable, the database refuses `app.set_custom_values` for the serial number (`QM033`) but accepts it sent back unchanged, and with the schedule off it is free again. **A lesson from it (`lib/editConflicts.js`):** the person's own saves are remembered per resource (`recordPath`), not per id — a record and its `custom_field_values` row written in one transaction (a new record, an import) have the same version text, so a custom value saved in a dialog made the record's own save look like a follow-on of it and drew a spurious 409 (retried silently). **Not built:** register row actions showing the lock up front (the database refusal and its message show), part revisions and order lines, a per-record override.
- **Imported non-conformances carry the source's stage** (`docs/cost-import-plan.md` §7, decisions CI-D9, CI-D12, CI-D13; slice IN-NC; migration `20260420100000_nc_import_stage.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it leaves the new fields refused as unknown on a mapping and the importer call failing, because `app.system_import_record` now passes the connection): the NC import catalogue gains **Stage** (the eight stages; empty = draft, as before; translate the source's words with the mapping's `map` step), **Closed on** (a date; empty = the import day; not in the future; not before the detected date **when the source gave one**) and **Reason cancelled** (empty = "Cancelled in <the connection's code>"); a value for a field that does not apply to the stage is ignored. **Rules:** a person still cannot insert anything but a draft or open NC; the importer sets the transaction-local switch **`app.ncr_history_import`** (only `app.import_nonconformance`, only for a stage other than draft; a refused row rolls it back with its savepoint) which `nonconformance_rules` honours on insert (any stage, `closed_at` kept, `closed_by` null). **The opening rules apply to every stage past draft except cancelled** (`app.assert_ncr_openable`: severity, quantity affected, a part or a description, a category, the customer / supplier the source names): a row without them is refused with the reason, and a mapping field's "when empty" default fills what the source lacks (there is no separate default-category setting). **The cumulative stage gates are a BEFORE UPDATE trigger, so an insert skips them**: a historical closed NC has no containment or disposition records, as the grandfathered ones; once in Fortress it is ordinary (reopen, then the gates apply to close). **Stages past open and before cancelled (contained … closed) need `ncr.approve_disposition` as well as `ncr.create`** (open and cancelled need only the latter), because they skip what those stages prove. **History is not news:** such an NC raises **no outbox event** (so no workflow rule starts: `app.nonconformance_events`) and **no notice** (`app.emit_notification` skips `nonconformances` while the switch is on); the audit trail is written as for any record (the person who started the import is the actor). A draft import is unchanged (it still raises `ncr.created`). NCs stay **add-only**: a later status change in the source is not carried. `app.import_nonconformance(org, id, data, connection)` replaced the three-argument form. The shared functions were patched with `pg_temp.patch` from their live definitions (`nonconformance_rules`, `nonconformance_events`, `emit_notification`, `system_import_record`). API: `catalogue.ts` fields `stage`, `closedOn`, `stageReason`; `columns.ts` maps them (`stage`, `closed_at`, `stage_reason`; `closed_by` is listed as never imported). Tests `supabase/tests/nc_import_stage.test.sql` (four deliberate breaks caught: notices, events, the permission, the opening rules), `integration-imports.e2e-spec.ts` (a CSV with Done / Live / Void statuses), `integration-catalogue-drift.e2e-spec.ts`. **Next:** the cost import (IC1–IC4) lets cost lines be added to closed or cancelled NCs an import brought in.
- **Importing NC cost lines, IC1 (database), IC2 (API) and IC3 (screens)** (`docs/cost-import-plan.md` §8–§10; migration `20260421100000_ncr_cost_import.sql`, applied live 2026-11-22 and checked; **IC4 done: `npm run journey` has a cost import step (`costImport()`: non-conformances in with their stages, a five-line file in four currencies previewed and imported, one line waiting for a rate and filled in by the worker, one Actual from the booked value, the credit rejected) and the exit report is `docs/cost-import-exit-report.md`; the cost import is complete**): new importable kind `ncr_cost` (add only, never matched, nothing disappears). `app.import_ncr_cost` writes a line through the table's own triggers (amount, VAT and both exchange rates are worked out on the line's own incurred date, in its own currency; a currency with no published rate still imports as `needed`), checks `ncr.update` at the NC's site itself (the table's insert rule does not apply inside a definer function), refuses a negative amount (a credit is a supplier recovery or customer charge) and takes an optional booked value through `app.override_cost_rate` (the line becomes Actual). The link to the NC is a reference kind `nonconformance`: the source's id through the import map, or the Fortress reference. **Closed and cancelled NCs refuse cost lines unless the NC was brought in by an import AND the cost template's `costsOnClosed` setting is on** (`app.ncr_cost_import_may_edit`, honoured by `app.assert_ncr_editable` only with the integration source, the map row and a transaction-local switch; a deleted NC never). `app.integration_cost_run_summary(run)` answers what will wait for a rate and what lands on closed or cancelled NCs. **IC2 (API, `docs/cost-import-plan.md` §9; screens are IC3):** catalogue kind `ncr_cost` with the link `nonconformance` (a new `RefKind`), `columns.ts` map, importer registered, add only; template setting `costsOnClosed` (`TemplateSettingsBody`, carried by template versions and a run's frozen settings); **the engine fetches the exchange rates a cost run's staged lines need before writing any** (`SyncEngine.warmRates` → `app.system_integration_cost_rates_needed` → `ExchangeRateService.ensurePairs`; a source that is down never fails the run, lines import as waiting) and settles once more when the run finishes; run detail carries `costs` (the summary). Test `api/test/integration-cost-import.e2e-spec.ts`. **Technique:** `pg_temp.patch` now strips `\r` first (live functions applied from Windows editors have CRLF; the NC stage migration failed on that once). Test `supabase/tests/ncr_cost_import.test.sql`. **IC3 (screens, no migration):** the wizard offers **NC cost lines** like any other kind (tiles come from the API's entity list; `ADD_ONLY_KINDS`, so write mode is fixed), `templateSettings` keeps `costsOnClosed` for a cost template only and `TemplateSettingsCard` has the switch **Allow costs on closed or cancelled non-conformances**, the link to the NC has Match by with its own words (`matchHint`: Fortress reference such as SNC-26-0011, or the id from importing the NCs through this connection first), `CostCurrencyNotice` explains in the mapping step that nothing is converted in the file, and **`CostRunCard` on the run page** (`dashboard`-style table from `run.costs`, preview or finished: per currency lines / with a rate / waiting, "N lines will wait for a rate", lines still waiting after the import, counts on closed and cancelled NCs, booked values) is where the currency summary lives (the template preview has no rates); the allowed-values dialog lists NCs and marks closed or cancelled ones. Tests `integrations/CostImport.test.jsx`. **A line with no date** (`docs/cost-import-plan.md` §12, CI-D14; migration `20260422100000_ncr_cost_missing_date.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it leaves templates with the setting unaware of it): the cost template setting `missingDate` = `reject` (default) | `nc_detected` — an empty date, or a file with no date column mapped, either refuses the line or dates it the day its NC was detected, at that day's rate (future → today); **the import day is deliberately not offered**, and a line with its own date keeps it. `catalogueForSettings` (`core/catalogue.ts`) makes `incurredOn` optional while it is on (template save, preview, engine); `app.import_ncr_cost` takes the setting (sixth argument, via `app.system_import_record`); `app.system_integration_run_settings` (API login only) lets the engine read a run's frozen settings; the run summary carries `datedFromNc` and the rate pre-fetch looks up the detected day. UI: "When a line has no date" combo on the template settings card, `fieldsForSettings` in `lib/importTemplates.js`, a note on `CostRunCard`. Caveat: an NC imported without "Detected on" has the import day as detected date. Tests `ncr_cost_import.test.sql`, `integration-cost-import.e2e-spec.ts`, `CostImport.test.jsx`.
- **Migration history** (reconciled 2026-11-23): every file in `supabase/migrations/` is applied live (checked object by object). Migrations applied through the SQL editor left no row in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`; the 24 missing ones were added with the files' own versions, so the table's versions can differ from the files' for earlier ones (those were applied with their own timestamps; the names match). The table is a record, not what decides anything: the app does not use the Supabase CLI to push. After applying a migration through the editor, add its row (`insert into supabase_migrations.schema_migrations (version, name) values ('<file version>', '<file name without version>')`).
- **Go-live status** (checked 2026-11-23; the single list to work from before the first paying client; update it as items are done):
  - **Done and verified.** Every migration in `supabase/migrations/` is applied live and the history table matches (see **Migration history**). Live guardrails: 0 violations. **Production API exists** (Vercel project `qms-api`, `api.fortress-qms.com`, deployed from `main`, `/api/v1/health` answers; no deployment or password protection in front of it), the app is `app.fortress-qms.com`, the website `fortress-qms.com`. **The billing gate is on** (`platform_settings.billing_gate_enabled = true`): new organisations must subscribe. **Stripe sandbox, end to end:** 6 GBP prices with their ids in `subscription_prices`, one billing customer, checkout, trial, subscription and invoice events processed (13 processed, 0 failed, `invoice.upcoming` ignored by design). Found 2026-11-23: the **production webhook endpoint had no `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`** (it was set for Preview and Development only), so the sandbox subscription's first renewal invoice (£85, `in_1UeBSgK5lGCrs5SiVu2gWuQM`) stayed a draft with Stripe waiting on the endpoint; fixed by creating a sandbox endpoint for `https://api.fortress-qms.com/api/v1/stripe/webhook` (`we_1UeumKK5lGCrs5SiALkRZSCe`), setting its secret on the Production API (sensitive variable) and redeploying; the old production endpoint (`we_1UcrD4K5lGCrs5Sif1TWXsrT`) is **disabled** (delete it in the Stripe dashboard; this connector cannot delete endpoints) and the Dev endpoint (`we_1UZ70FK5lGCrs5SiLECQeaW0`) still points at the Dev alias. After the fix `invoice.finalized` and `invoice.updated` were received and processed. **The live Stripe account (`Fortress QMS`, `acct_1UZ4npGSg4BqbNAb`) now mirrors the sandbox's catalogue**: three products named Fortress QMS Observer / Contributor / Controller (`plan_slug` metadata, tax code `txcd_10103001`), six GBP prices with lookup keys `fortress_<plan>_month|year` (monthly £5 / £10 / £25, annual ten times, `tax_behavior: exclusive`; ids: Observer `price_1UZ5FtGSg4BqbNAbFkAlOGCA` / `price_1Uev06GSg4BqbNAbTovZtOZQ`, Contributor `price_1UZ5FtGSg4BqbNAbBrbNavF6` / `price_1Uev09GSg4BqbNAbi3rCSb8i`, Controller `price_1UZ5FtGSg4BqbNAbI0lcdQ9M` / `price_1Uev0CGSg4BqbNAbjwYu4jge`), the old one-time price archived, and a Customer Portal configuration (`bpc_1Uev1rGSg4BqbNAbnQdfDryb`, same as the sandbox's: details, payment method, invoices, cancel at period end, no plan changes). The live account has no customers, subscriptions or webhook for production. Stripe Tax settings are identical in both accounts (GB head office, no registrations).
  - **Still to do before the first paying client** (in this order; the owner does what needs their accounts):
    1. **Confirm the sandbox invoice is paid** (`in_1UeBSgK5lGCrs5SiVu2gWuQM` was finalised 2026-11-23 10:51 UTC and was still open with no attempt): expect `invoice.paid` / `invoice.payment_succeeded` in `stripe_events`, a `billing_invoices` and `billing_payments` row and the invoice `paid` in Stripe. That is the proof the production webhook verifies signatures. Also run the rest of `docs/stripe-setup.md` §5 on the production deployment (seat change with preview, cancel at period end, a failing card, the Customer Portal, a webhook retry).
    2. **One database, two Stripe modes.** Dev and Production share one Supabase project, and `subscription_prices.provider_price_id` holds one Stripe price id per price. **Decide before launch:** a second Supabase project for production (cleanest), or switch the ids to the live ones at launch (Dev then cannot be tested with sandbox keys). Today Production runs on **sandbox keys**.
    3. **Switch Production to live Stripe:** create the live webhook for `https://api.fortress-qms.com/api/v1/stripe/webhook` (events as in `docs/stripe-setup.md`), set `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (live) and `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (live) **together** on the Production API, write the live price ids above into `subscription_prices`, redeploy, delete or disable the live webhook that points at the Dev alias, and run one real £ charge on a card of your own and refund it. Add a Stripe Tax registration if you are VAT registered. Prices are excluding VAT.
    4. **Email:** `MAIL_PROVIDER` / `MAIL_FROM` / `RESEND_API_KEY` are set on the API for all three environments (value of `MAIL_PROVIDER` not checked); confirm the sending domain is verified in Resend and that an invitation, a verification and a notification email arrive and are not filtered. Supabase's auth templates must be the ones in `supabase/templates/` and its Redirect URLs must allow `app.fortress-qms.com/**`.
    5. **Supabase Auth:** turn on **leaked password protection** (needs the Pro plan, as does SAML single sign-on, which is still not live-tested); set the Site URL (`https://app.fortress-qms.com`) and check the redirect URLs; rotate any key the audit report lists.
    6. **Operators:** add yourself to `platform_admins`, enrol two-factor, open every `/platform` tab. The worker needs `supabase/runbooks/worker-schedule.sql` applied with the production tick address (see **Background worker**).
    7. **Legal and support:** the website's legal texts stay drafts until `PUBLIC_LEGAL_REVIEWED=true` (reviewed terms, privacy, a data processing agreement); decide a support route, take a backup rehearsal (Supabase point-in-time recovery on, one restore tried).
    8. **Browser checks the journey does not cover:** mobile layouts, Safari and Firefox on the screens a client uses first (NC, inspection, job hub, calibration, the portal); the external portal and the CMM readers have never been looked at in a browser or against real files.
    9. **Not needed for a first client, so leave until asked:** the Windows connector agent against a real SQL Server (no Windows machine tried yet; the signing certificate is still to buy), SAML live check, the portal journey step, notices when a scheduled import's filter leaves out everything.
  - **Tools used for the Stripe work** (so a later session knows what it can do): the Stripe connector can read everything and write products, prices, webhook endpoints (create and disable, not delete), portal configurations and invoice finalise; it cannot pay an invoice or resend an event. Vercel's connector sets environment variables and redeploys; sensitive values can never be read back, so check a variable by its presence and creation time, not its value.
- **Reporting API for Power BI** (`docs/reporting-api-plan.md`, decisions R1–R5 agreed 2026-11-23; slices RP1–RP4 built; migration `20260425100000_reporting_keys.sql`, applied live 2026-11-23 (checked: table, six `security_invoker` views, three functions, the lookup callable by the API login only, history row present); pushing the API before it breaks only the reporting routes): a read-only way for a customer to load their data into Power BI (import mode, scheduled refresh, no gateway) with a long-lived key instead of a person's short sign-in. **Keys:** `reporting_keys` (`fqr_` + 32 random bytes, shown once; only the SHA-256 and an eight-character prefix are stored; expires in 1–730 days, default 365; revoked with a reason, never deleted; last used time and address kept; at most ten live per organisation; functions-only writes, audited, RLS `integrations.read`) made by `app.create_reporting_key` and revoked by `app.revoke_reporting_key` (**`integrations.manage`, so a Controller seat; no new permission or package feature**). **A key acts as the person who made it, read-only:** `ReportKeyAuth` (`api/src/reporting/report-key-auth.ts`, called from `AuthGuard`) turns `Authorization: Bearer fqr_…` or `Basic base64(<any user>:<key>)` (Power BI's Basic credential, so the key never sits in a report file) into that person through `app.system_report_key_lookup` (API login only; nothing for an unknown, revoked or expired key or a person who is no longer an active member), sets `req.user` (role authenticated, **`aal: aal2` because making a key needed a two-factor session, and no `amr`, so single sign-on and session-age rules do not apply to a key; its own expiry does**: `OrganisationGuard` skips the session-age check when `req.reportKey` is set) and overwrites `X-Organisation-Id` with the key's own organisation, **so every existing check still runs: membership, two-factor, seat, the network rule, permission, row-level security**. **A key can call nothing but `GET /reporting/v1/*`:** a route must carry `@ReportingRoute()` for `AuthGuard` to accept a key, anything else is `403 report_key_scope` (so a leaked key cannot write, make keys or read the rest of the API); bad keys are `401 report_key_invalid` (no hint which part is wrong) and 30 failures a minute from one address are `429`. **Datasets** are plain views in `public` (`report_non_conformances`, `report_non_conformance_costs`, `report_suppliers`, `report_scars`, `report_receipt_lines`, `report_supplier_scorecards`; `security_invoker = true`, LEFT JOINs for names so a missing lookup right blanks a name instead of dropping a row; costs are signed (`net_organisation_currency`, credits negative) and the NC view carries `total_cost`; the SCAR view leaves drafts out). `api/src/reporting/datasets.ts` is the contract (columns in the view's order with a type; **`reporting.e2e-spec.ts` fails when it and a view differ; a new column is added to both on purpose**): `selectList` casts every value (number → float, date → `YYYY-MM-DD`, datetime → ISO UTC with microseconds, json nested) so the JSON is the same whatever the driver does. API `GET /reporting/v1` (and `/v1/datasets`: the list, which also answers 200 to a connection test), `GET /reporting/v1/{dataset}?limit=2000|≤5000&after=<cursor>&updatedSince=<ISO UTC>` → `{ dataset, asOf, count, nextCursor, data }` (keyset on `(updated_at, id)`, oldest change first, the dataset's read permission checked first so a person without it gets `403`), and for people only `GET|POST /reporting/keys`, `POST /reporting/keys/{id}/revoke`. UI: Settings → Integrations → **Power BI and reporting** (`integrations/PowerBiCard.jsx`: keys with prefix, who they act as and last use; Make a key (shown once, with Basic sign-in advice), Revoke with a reason (optimistic), **Connect Power BI** = the address, the steps and a generated Power Query script per dataset that pages through `nextCursor`, types the columns and keeps the key out of the file); hooks `useReporting.js`, `lib/powerBi.js` (`powerQueryScript`). Tests `supabase/tests/reporting_keys.test.sql` (+ sweep seed, guardrails list), `api/src/reporting/report-key.spec.ts`, `api/test/reporting.e2e-spec.ts` (+ API sweep: the revoke route and a skip for `{dataset}`), `integrations/PowerBi.test.jsx`. **Not done:** tried in a real Power BI Desktop or Service (the script and paging are tested here, the first client's setup is the check), OData, DirectQuery (needs a relational source; possible later on single-tenant deployments over the same views), Power BI's own incremental refresh set-up, inspections, measurements, 8D, audits, risks and calibration datasets (same pattern), key use notices or email, a per-key dataset choice. NC `updated_at` also moves with its cost lines, but a change to a part or supplier name does not move the rows that show it: a full refresh picks it up. **A reporting key's `user_id` is classified `history` in `app.user_reference_columns()`** (migration `20260427100000_reporting_key_user_reference.sql`, applied live 2026-11-23 (checked: the row is in `app.user_reference_columns()`); it was missed when keys were added and `sso_joining_moves.test.sql` caught it): when a person is moved onto their company account their key keeps naming the old account, which is no longer an active member, so it stops working and a new one is made. It fails safe; nothing changes which identity a credential acts as.
- **Record filters for imports** (`docs/import-filters-plan.md`, decisions F-D1–F-D8 agreed 2026-11-23; slices F1–F4; **F1 built**, migration `20260423100000_import_filters.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it breaks the runs endpoints (they select `filtered`) and leaves a template's filter unread): which source records an import reads at all. A filter (`core/filters.ts`: rules `column, test, value` all / any, keep or leave out, or one formula; ≤ 20 rules) is kept in the template version's `settings.filters` (frozen with it, copied into the run's settings) and applied by the sync engine to the **raw source record before mapping**; a record it leaves out is staged `skipped` + `filtered` with "Filtered out: …" and counted in `runs.filtered` (a subset of `skipped`); a formula that fails rejects the row ("Filter formula: …") instead of dropping it. **A filtered record is never "no longer in the source"**: its own identifier is kept in `integration_inbox.source_external_id` and `app.integration_detect_missing` counts it as present. `app.integration_template_settings` is now a wrapper over `integration_template_settings_core` that validates (`app.integration_filter_problem`) and keeps `filters`. API: `settings.filters` on template bodies and versions (checked against the expected columns, tidied), `POST /integrations/templates/filter-preview` (`integrations.run`: every row of an uploaded file counted, an API's / database's sample, per-rule counts, five of each), `filteredOut` per row on `templates/preview`. Frontend `templateSettings()` carries `filters` so a new version never drops one. **F2 built (screens, no migration):** the file wizard now has **four steps — Choose records, Upload a file, Filter records, Map columns** (the mapping is step 4; `wizardPath` callers, the run page's "Change the mapping" link and the old `/mapping` redirect all use `step: 4`), and API / connector-agent mapping pages (`ApiMappingPage.jsx`) have a **Filter records** card above the mapping. `integrations/FilterBuilder.jsx` is the one rule builder (what to read: only matching / all but matching; all-or-any; per rule column combo from the source's real columns, test, value / list (one per line) / from-to, "read as" number or date with decimal mark / date format for ordered tests; **Use a formula instead of rules** only with advanced imports; **Remove the filter**) with `FilterResult` under it: the server's "N of M records kept", whether it counted every row or only the sample an API / database sends, per-rule counts, problems, a "keeps nothing" warning, a formula that fails for some records, and a few records of each kind. `lib/importFilters.js` holds the words, `cleanFilter` (only complete rules go to the server and into the saved template, so **a half-typed rule never makes a template look changed**: `templateSettings` runs it), `filterWords`; hook `useFilterPreview` (debounced, `POST /integrations/templates/filter-preview`). The draft lives in `form.settings.filters`; the mapping step shows the filter in words with **Change the filter**, and the mapping preview and the saved template version carry it. Tests `lib/importFilters.test.js`, `IntegrationFiles.test.jsx` (step 3), `ApiMapping.test.jsx`; the journey's three file imports click through the filter step. **F3 built (run page, warnings, health; migration `20260424100000_import_filters_run_page.sql`, applied live (checked 2026-11-23); pushing the API before it breaks only `?filtered=true` on a run's rows and leaves the health card without the filter flag):** `app.integration_run_filtered_rows(run, limit, offset)` / `app.integration_run_filtered_count(run)` (one permission check, a partial index `integration_inbox_filtered_idx`) give the records a filter left out without reading the whole run; `GET /integrations/runs/{id}/rows?filtered=true` uses them and every row carries `filtered`. The run page has a **Filtered out** tile (with the share left out; **Skipped** now holds only what the import settings left alone), a **Filtered out** choice in the rows bar listing each record with the reason it was left out (a skipped row's reason is shown in grey, not as an error), and a warning `Callout` when the filter left out every record or kept under a tenth of 10 or more (`keepsLittle` in `lib/importFilters.js`, also used by the filter step's preview: **a warning, never a block**). `app.integration_health` (patched from its live text) puts `recordsTotal` and `filtered` on `lastRun`; the API adds `filterLeftOut` = `all` (every record read was left out) or `most` (at least 90% of 10 or more) and treats either as **attention**; the health card says "the filter left out every record read (N)" / "…X% of the records read". Tests `supabase/tests/import_filters.test.sql` (extended), `integration-filters.e2e-spec.ts`, `IntegrationFiles.test.jsx`, `KeepHealthy.test.jsx`, `lib/importFilters.test.js`. **F4 done (exit check, `docs/import-filters-exit-report.md`, no migration):** live guardrail rules checked (0 violations), advisors nothing new for these objects, and **`npm run journey` has a record filters step** (`recordFilters()`: four machines imported through the wizard with a filter on Status — the filter step warns that a value nobody has keeps nothing, then says "2 of 4 records kept"; the import writes only the active machines; the run page says 50% were left out and lists them with the reason; the template version keeps the filter). **Record filters are complete.** Not built: notices or email when a scheduled import's filter leaves out everything (the health card flags it), a filter on what Fortress already holds. **Journey notes:** the file wizard's mapping is now step 4 (every journey import clicks Next through the filter step), and a new file import should start `?template=new` when an earlier template names columns the file lacks. Tests `core/filters.spec.ts`, `supabase/tests/import_filters.test.sql`, `api/test/integration-filters.e2e-spec.ts`.
- **Quality alert register** (no migration): `/Documentation/Quality-Alerts` is now the full register like the 8D and NC ones — `TableWrapper` with server sort and paging, columns in `documentation/alertColumns.jsx` (`useTablePreferences("quality-alert-register")`; alert, title, category, severity, status, effective, expires and, for `documentation.manage`, acknowledged are shown; acknowledge-by, issued, issued by, drafted by and updated start hidden), the header search box feeds `search` (reference and title), the right-hand filter drawer `AlertFilterPane.jsx` (status, category, severity pills, overdue acknowledgements for managers, effective and expiry date ranges; URL keys `QUALITY_ALERT_FILTER_KEYS`) with removable chips, CSV export of selected rows, row menu. `GET /quality-alerts` is page-based now: comma-separated `status` (none or `all` = the newest revision of each alert; named states list every revision in them), `category`, `severity`, `from|to` (effective date), `expiresFrom|expiresTo`, `overdue`, `q`, `sort` (`reference|title|category|severity|status|effectiveDate|expiryDate|acknowledgeBy|issuedAt|updatedAt`), `order`, `page`, `pageSize` (`limit` is the older name, default 100); answers `{ data, page, pageSize, total }`. Tests in `process-control.e2e-spec.ts` and `ProcessControl.test.jsx`.
- **Header command palette** (`docs/command-palette-plan.md`, agreed 2026-11-23; no migration): the header search bar still filters the register or list on screen as you type; **Ctrl/⌘+K or the `Ctrl K` button in the bar opens a palette** (`components/palette/CommandPalette.jsx`, open state, shortcut and recents in `contexts/CommandPaletteProvider.jsx` mounted in `App.jsx`) that starts empty, leaves the bar's text alone and closes with Esc. It finds **pages** (the side menu and Settings, built from `MenuStructure.js` / `SettingMenuStructure.js` with the menu's own visibility rules, so a new menu entry is found by itself), **actions** (`ROUTE_ACTIONS` in `lib/commandPalette.js`: one line each, with the permission needed; theme and sign out run on the spot) and **records** (`GET /search?q&kind&limit`, `api/src/search/search.service.ts`: non-conformances, 8Ds, parts, suppliers, gauges, inspections, documents; from two letters; **a kind the caller may not read is absent from the answer, never zero**; read as the caller so RLS still limits rows; `%` and `_` are literal), grouped under chips with counts and marked matches, ↑ ↓ ↵ esc. **Recently opened** (shown when empty) lives in this browser (`fortress.recent.<user>.<organisation>`, newest eight): filled by what is chosen and by every record page that names itself for the breadcrumb. A new record kind needs its finder in `FINDERS`, its permission in `SearchService.find`, and entries in `SEARCH_KINDS` and `RECORD_KINDS`. Tests `lib/commandPalette.test.js`, `CommandPalette.test.jsx`, `api/test/search.e2e-spec.ts` (+ the tenancy sweep's `?q=00`). **Not built:** more kinds, searching other fields, recents that follow the person across devices, a journey step; not browser-checked.
- **Audit fixes (docs/audit-2026-11.md; migrations `20260124100000_calibration_integrity.sql`, `20260125100000_integrity_rules.sql` and `20260126100000_business_rules.sql`, applied live 2026-11-11):**
  - **Calibration (H1–H3):** `POST /calibrations/{id}/raise-ncr` locks the calibration row first (one NC per failed calibration); deferred constraint trigger `calibration_readings_complete` (QM006: a reading for every check before saving; the API flushes it with `set constraints calibrations_after, calibration_readings_complete immediate`); after the creating transaction, changing date/outcome/readings/deleted needs `calibration.manage` (`calibration_correction_guard`); notes and the NC link stay open.
  - **NC and members (H4, H6, M9, M10, M12, M13):** `nonconformances_keep_opening_fields` (opening fields cannot be blanked past draft), stronger `app.ncr_gate_problems` (severity, quantity affected, waiver reason ≥ 10 characters; caller needs `ncr.read`), the organisation currency cannot change once NC costs exist (QM014), a removed member can be invited again (partial unique index on `organisation_users`, status rules in `app.organisation_user_status_rules`, QM015), NC owner / detected by and gauge custodian must be active members, an NC cannot be deleted while a running 8D links it.
  - **`/me` permissions (H5):** `app.my_visible_permissions(org)` = permissions held at any scope (menus only); `app.my_permissions` stays organisation-wide for grantable rights; the server still decides per site.
  - **Business rules (`20260126100000`):** purchase orders refuse suspended / disqualified / archived suppliers (QM021), a supplier with open orders cannot be switched off (QM020), a lot cannot be released or closed and an order cannot be completed or closed while an open NC names it (QM017; an approver's conditional acceptance of the delivery is the explicit concession and may release the lot), a new production order needs an active part (not for imported orders).
  - **API hardening (M1–M4):** `helmet` headers; Swagger docs and `/openapi.json` are **off in production** unless `API_DOCS=true` (default on elsewhere, `docsEnabled(env)`); `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` (default 1 in production, 0 elsewhere) so the rate limit sees the caller's address behind Vercel; an oversized body answers `413 payload_too_large`; root `vercel.json` adds security headers and a report-only Content-Security-Policy. Test `api/test/hardening.e2e-spec.ts`.
  - **Frontend (M6, M11):** dead direct-Supabase code removed (`src/api/supabaseApi.js` keeps only `uploadFileToSupabase` / `generateSignedUrl`; the exchange-rate API, `useNCKPI`, `useNCDateTrend`, `useSupabaseSearch`, the old charts and `VITE_UNIRATE_API_KEY` are gone). Settings → Account is real: personal details save through `PATCH /me`, the password form is masked, checks the current password by signing in again, then `updatePassword` (same rules as sign-up); Delete account is an explanation (nothing is deleted). Test `SettingsAccount.test.jsx`.
  - **Dates and time zones (M14, no migration):** the browser sends its IANA zone as `X-Time-Zone` on every API call (`api-client.js`); `contextFrom` validates it (`validTimeZone`, UTC when absent or unknown, `ctx.timeZone`) and `DatabaseService.asUser` runs the request with `set_config('TimeZone', …)`, so **`current_date`, `::date` casts and `date_trunc` in every function and query are the person's own day** ("not in the future" checks, overdue, expiry, dashboards). JS "today" in the API is `todayIn(ctx.timeZone)` (`supplier-quality/expiry.ts`; `todayUtc` is only for tests/system code); crons and `asApi` stay UTC. The frontend never uses `toISOString().slice(0, 10)` for today: use `todayIso(days?)`, `localDay(date)`, `thisMonthIso()` from `src/lib/localDate.js`. Tests `request-context.spec.ts`, `expiry.spec.ts`, `api.e2e-spec.ts`. **Time zone picker** (`src/lib/timeZones.js`, `ui/TimeZoneCombo.jsx`): the list is bundled (every current IANA name from tzdata, 418; browsers' `Intl.supportedValuesOf` lists the old `Asia/Calcutta` and has no `Asia/Kolkata`), each option shows its current offset beneath it (`UTC+05:30`, daylight saving included) and the search matches the name, the offset (`+5:30`) and the name without underscores (`RHFComboBox` options take `keywords`); a site that holds an older name keeps it. A person's zone is not stored: it is whatever their browser says (site time zones are still only for display/labelling).
  - **Not fixed (see the report):** M5 leaked-password protection and the M6 key rotation (Supabase / UniRate dashboards), M8 locked-organisation master-data reads, manual quarantine release reason, next-due cap, over-receipt and lot-vs-order quantity limits, leaver reassignment.
- **Competence** (`docs/competence-plan.md`, decisions CP1–CP17 agreed; slices CO1–CO5; **CO1 done**, migration `20260127100000_competence_core.sql`, applied live 2026-11-11; pushing the API before it leaves the competence endpoints failing): the module for who is competent at what; **information only** (never blocks work), people come from the Employees register, fixed 0–4 scale (none / learning / supervised / competent / can train others), required skills come from **positions**, assessments need `competence.assess` and never your own, per-skill refresh computed on read (CO2+). Name **Competence**, icon `GraduationCap` (`GraduationCapIcon`). **CO1 = set-up only:** permissions `competence.read` / `.manage` / `.assess` (owner, admin, quality manager all three; quality engineer read + assess; inspector and viewer none), **Controller-seat holders read everything** (`app.competence_seat_read`; `app.has_permission`, `has_permission_somewhere`, `my_permissions` and `my_visible_permissions` grant `competence.read` to an active member holding a Controller seat, so RLS, the API guard and the menu follow; reading only), `competence_categories` (seeded per organisation: Inspection, Measurement, Machine operation, Quality system, Health and safety, Other; direct RLS inserts/updates under `competence.manage`, key fixed, nothing deleted), `competence_skills` (code fixed once created and unique ignoring case, name unique among active, category, optional `refresh_months` 1–120, retired with a reason, never deleted, **cannot be retired while an active position requires it**) and `competence_positions` (same shape) and `competence_position_skills` (skill + required level 1–4, **replaced as a whole** by `app.set_position_skills`; only active skills). Skills and positions are written **only by SECURITY DEFINER functions** (`app.save_competence_skill|position(org, jsonb, id?)`, `retire_ / reactivate_competence_skill|position`, `set_position_skills`; `competence.manage`; errors `QM024` → `409 competence_rule`, another organisation `P0002` → 404). API `api/src/competence/` under `/competence`: `GET|POST categories`, `PATCH categories/{id}`, `GET|POST skills` (`all=true` includes retired, `categoryId`, `q`), `PATCH skills/{id}`, `POST skills/{id}/retire|reactivate`, `GET|POST positions`, `GET|PATCH positions/{id}`, `PUT positions/{id}/skills {skills:[{skillId, requiredLevel}]}`, `POST positions/{id}/retire|reactivate`. UI: menu item **Competence** (`/Competence`, `CompetenceHome.jsx`: read-only skills by category and what each position requires, with a link to set-up) and **Settings → Data Management → Competence** (`Settings/SettingsCompetence.jsx`: categories with on/off, skills grouped by category with retired ones folded away, positions with **Required skills** dialog, retire with a reason), hooks `useCompetence.js`, helpers `lib/competence.js` (`LEVELS`, `levelLabel`, `refreshLabel`, `groupByCategory`, `requirementSummary`).
