a tick that finds it taken is recorded as skipped), then each step on its own (a failure is recorded, the rest still run): quality alerts expire, documents settle, **due jobs run**, email is fanned out and sent, waiting import runs are worked; `app.system_worker_end` records the steps and clears ticks after 7 days, finished jobs after 30 (failed 90). **Jobs:** `app.worker_jobs` (kind, payload, unique idempotency key, run at; leased `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; attempts counted at lease, so a job whose worker died is due again and still stops; failures back off 1, 4, 16, 64 minutes, final after `max_attempts`); handlers register by kind in `WorkerJobRegistry` (`onModuleInit`), throw `PermanentJobError` to fail at once, unknown kinds fail with "No handler"; queue with `WorkerService.enqueue(kind, payload, { organisationId, idempotencyKey, runAt })` or `app.system_enqueue_job` from a SECURITY DEFINER trigger. **Outbox consumers:** `app.system_outbox_read(consumer, limit)` / `app.system_outbox_ack(consumer, ids)` — a delivery row per consumer and event (not an id cursor: identity ids can commit out of order); a consumer sees only events written after it first read. Everything is in the `app` schema behind API-login-only functions; operators see `GET /platform/worker` (`app.platform_worker_health`: ticks with steps, ticks in the last hour, jobs by kind and status, failures with errors — never payloads, consumers' backlogs) on the **Worker** tab of `/platform`. The daily Vercel crons stay as a safety net. Tests `supabase/tests/worker_foundation.test.sql`, `api/test/worker.e2e-spec.ts`, `PlatformPanels.test.jsx`. **WX done** (exit check, `docs/workflow-engine-exit-report.md`; the journey has a workflow and reminders step, `stack.tick()` runs one worker tick): the workflow engine plan is complete. Scheduled supplier scorecards were built afterwards (SQ5b, below).
- **Gauge R&R (measurement system analysis), MS1 to MS6 built (exit report `docs/gauge-rr-exit-report.md`)** (`docs/gauge-rr-plan.md`, decisions D1–D14 agreed 2026-11-24; slices MS1–MS6; **API and screens are built**): a "Gauge R&R" item under Calibration for crossed gauge R&R (ANOVA and average-and-range), Type 1 (Cg / Cgk) and attribute agreement studies, information only (a poor result never changes a gauge or blocks recording), reusing `calibration.read|record|manage` (no new permission), appraisers from the Employees register, a randomised run sheet plus paste / CSV, frozen on completion. **MS1 = `api/src/msa/`**: pure statistics (`analyseCrossed`, `analyseType1`, `analyseAttribute`; `ALGORITHM_VERSION` `msa-1`, criteria id `aiag-msa-4`), no database. Verified against the AIAG MSA 4th edition worked example, statsmodels / scipy (`reference/generate.py` → `reference.json`; not product dependencies; the generator takes about five minutes) and a variance-recovery simulation, with 18 deliberate mistakes tried. Plan §11 lists what was checked against public sources after MS1 and what is **still not verified** (the attribute bands against the manual's own table; VDA 5): "each appraiser vs standard" kappa now follows Minitab's published definition (per trial, the standard as a second rater, averaged over trials). **Never change a formula without its spec; a result is frozen with its algorithm version.** **MS2 = migration `20260428100000_gauge_rr.sql` (applied live 2026-11-24; the history table row is the owner's to add)**: `msa_studies` (`MSA-YY-NNNN`, `grr_crossed | type1 | attribute`, draft → collecting → completed | cancelled; the gauge's unit / resolution and the characteristic's name, unit and limits are copied; design editable only as a draft, locked once collecting, frozen when completed or cancelled even for SQL), `msa_study_appraisers` (Employees register or a typed name), `msa_study_parts` (label, attribute reference decision), `msa_study_measurements` (**one row per cell, made empty when collection starts with a repeatable random `run_order` per appraiser and trial; filled by `app.record_msa_measurements`, overwritable while collecting and audited, never deleted or cleared**; numbers kept as written) and `msa_study_results` (the frozen result jsonb, algorithm version, criteria id, verdict, `data_hash`). **Functions only** (`app.create_msa_study`, `update_msa_design`, `start_msa_collection`, `record_msa_measurements(study, cells[{appraiser, part, trial, value|decision}], 'grid'|'paste')`, `cancel_msa_study`, `repeat_msa_study`); read `calibration.read`, record `calibration.record`, design / start / cancel / repeat `calibration.manage`, all site-scoped through the gauge; `QM034` → `409 msa_rule`. **Completing is API-login only** (`app.system_complete_msa_study(org, study, person, conclusion, note, valid_until, result, data_hash)`): it acts as the person (still needs `calibration.manage` at the gauge's site), needs the study collecting with every cell filled, requires `data_hash` = SHA-256 hex of `app.msa_data_canonical(study)` (design and options, each part's reference decision, every cell in fixed order, numbers as stored — **the API must build the same text from the rows it read**), keeps the result (`algorithm`, `criteria`, `verdict.band`), freezes the study and writes outbox event `msa.study.completed`; valid for 12 months by default (expiry is computed on read). Tests `supabase/tests/gauge_rr.test.sql` (nine deliberate breaks tried: the hash check, empty cells, the person's right, the design lock, readings on a completed study, the frozen result, a viewer recording, the hash ignoring reference decisions, deleting a study), tenancy sweep seed, guardrails audited list. **MS3 = `api/src/msa/msa.*` + `msa-data.ts`** (plan §12): `GET|POST /msa/studies` (page-based), `GET|PATCH /msa/studies/{id}` (PATCH = the design of a draft; `@Versioned('msa_studies')`), `POST …/start|complete|cancel|repeat`, `PUT …/measurements`, `POST …/measurements/parse` (pasted text → cells, nothing saved), `GET …/analysis|report`, `GET /gauges/{id}/msa`; `QM034` → `409 msa_rule`. Completing reads the study as the person, works the result out in `analyseStudy`, hashes the data in TypeScript (`dataHash`, which must equal the database's `app.msa_data_canonical` — the e2e test checks both and recomputes a frozen result from its stored data), then calls the API-login function. A part + characteristic key brings its name, unit and limits from the part's released revision. Tests `msa-data.spec.ts`, `api/test/msa.e2e-spec.ts`, tenancy sweep. **MS4 done (screens, no migration; plan §13)**: menu Calibration → Gauge R&R (`/Calibration/Gauge-RR[/:id[/report]]`), `src/components/msa/` (register `MsaStudiesPage` in the usual register pattern, `StudyDialog`, `MsaStudyPage`, `RunSheet` (random run order, one box per reading saved on leaving or Enter, optimistic through `useRecordMeasurements`, "By part" layout, `PasteDialog` reading through `POST …/measurements/parse`), `AnalysisView` with charts, `MsaReportPage` for print, `GaugeStudies` card on the gauge page), `hooks/useMsa.js`, `lib/msa.js`, filter keys `MSA_FILTER_KEYS`; tests `Msa.test.jsx`, `lib/msa.test.js`, `refresh.test.js`; checked at 375 px in the browser with sample data only. **MS5 done (plan §14; migration `20260429100000_gauge_rr_workflow_reminders.sql`, to be applied live; pushing the API before it leaves only the workflow trigger and reminder area missing)**: `GET /msa/coverage?state&gaugeId&controlPlanId&characteristicKey` (`msa-coverage.ts` `coverageState`: critical and major characteristics of plans in force × gauges that recorded readings in 12 months × the latest completed study; states worst first `not_accepted`, `expired`, `no_study`, `not_judged`, `marginal`, `no_readings`, `acceptable`; `available: false` with the reason without `process_control.read` and `inspection.read`; information only), `/Calibration/Gauge-RR?view=coverage` (`MsaHome` toggle, `CoverageView`, **Plan a study** prefilled through `StudyDialog defaults`), a Gauge R&R badge per characteristic on the control plan revision page, workflow trigger `msa.study.completed` (22 triggers) and reminders area `msa_studies` (gauge custodians, 13 areas). Tests `msa-coverage.spec.ts`, `msa-coverage.e2e-spec.ts`, `gauge_rr_workflow_reminders.test.sql`. **MS6 done (exit check, no migration of its own):** live guardrails (the rules for the new objects) 0 violations and advisors nothing new (the two project-wide warnings); **`npm run journey` has a Gauge R&R step** (`gaugeRR()` in `scripts/journey/journey.cjs`: a critical diameter measured with a micrometer nobody has studied → coverage says No study → Plan a study from that row with the plan's limits in the form → crossed study, start, paste twelve readings, record, complete → the frozen result (`msa-1`, 64-character fingerprint) → report → coverage, the control plan badge and the gauge card all say Acceptable). The browser found three real faults, all fixed with tests: **an audit note typed straight after choosing Fail was wiped when the answer's placeholder id was swapped for the real one** (`AuditRecordPage.jsx` `Question` now follows the server only when the value or note changes), **Plan a study from coverage left the plan's limits empty** (coverage rows now carry nominal, limits and unit) and **a study naming a part and a characteristic written on the control plan was refused with `unknown_characteristic`** (`MsaService.characteristicDefaults` also looks on an effective revision of an active plan covering the part, for the family or for the single part; a key found nowhere is still refused). Not built: linearity and stability studies, files on a study, a notice when a study expires, configurable criteria; **the attribute bands against the AIAG manual's own table and VDA 5 are still unverified** (plan §11). **The MS5 migration `20260429100000_gauge_rr_workflow_reminders.sql` still has to be applied live** (then its history row).
- **Change management (ECR / ECN), CH1, CH2 and CH3 built, CH4–CH6 not** (`docs/change-management-plan.md`, decisions CH1–CH13; slices CH1–CH6 then CHX; **CH1 = migration `20260430100000_change_management_core.sql` (not yet applied live; pushing the API before it breaks every change endpoint and `/me`'s change permissions), see the plan's §8a**): one `change_requests` record (`ECR-YY-NNNN`, ECR while assessed, ECN once approved) above the existing revision lifecycles — origin (NC, 8D action, finding, risk, document change request, portal request), scope links, impact assessment by area, **board sign-off by function** (the raiser cannot sign), tasks **linked to the real part / control plan / document revision with its live status**, a **cut-in point** (date, order, lot or serial; recorded, never enforced), customer approval recorded, closing effectiveness check. **Informs by default; an opt-in gate** (`organisation_settings.change_gate_enabled`, CH5) can hold a named revision's release until its change is approved. Permissions `change.read` (view), `change.approve` (sign for a function; Contributor and up) and `change.manage` (everything else: Controller), errors `QM035` → `409 change_rule`. **Decided 2026-11-24 (plan §10):** the raiser may sign their own change only when `organisation_settings.change_allow_self_approval` is on (default off, Settings → System preferences); no emergency path; a small Changes section in the management review; the worker was set to hourly on 2026-11-24 (`cron.alter_job` on `fortress-worker-tick`, `0 * * * *`; the runbook still says every five minutes) to stay under Vercel Hobby's Active CPU allowance. **CH1 built:** module, permissions and package rows, lists seeded per organisation (`change_types` with `needs_customer_approval`, `change_functions`, `change_areas`), `change_requests` (a draft or cancelled; wording changes only in draft; origin and `raised_by` never change), `change_links` (scope, replaced as a whole), `change_attachments` (`change-files` bucket, 14 organisation buckets now), functions-only writes (`app.create_change|update_change|set_change_links|cancel_change|add_/remove_/restore_change_attachment`), API `api/src/change/` (+ `QM035`), UI `src/components/change/` (`/Change`, `/Change/:id`), Settings → Data Management → Change, menu item Change. Tests `change_management_core.test.sql`, `change.e2e-spec.ts` (+ sweeps), `Change.test.jsx`, `SettingsChange.test.jsx`. **Not built:** assessment, board, tasks, cut-in, customer approval, the gate, notices, dashboard, raise-from, Changes cards, the register's full pattern (column menu, filter drawer, saved filters), a journey step. **CH2 built (migration `20260431100000_change_assessment_approval.sql`, not yet applied live; pushing the API before it breaks the change detail and every organisation settings read, which select `change_allow_self_approval`):** submit (draft → assessment with a row per active impact area), impact assessment (`change_impacts`), the board (`change_board`: function + signer who holds `change.approve`, seat included), send to board (round N+1, `change_signoffs` one pending per function), decide (a rejection with a comment returns to assessment and supersedes the round; the last approval approves in the same transaction), recall (reason), cut-in point (none / date / order / lot / serial + stock action and note, set by a manager or a signer of the round) and the customer approval record (not_needed / required / requested / approved / rejected / waived with a reason), all recorded, never enforced; **the raiser cannot be on the board or sign unless `organisation_settings.change_allow_self_approval` is on** (default off, Settings → System preferences); stage moves held by `app.change_rules`; notices `change_signoffs.created` (to the signer) and `change_requests.updated` (to the raiser when approved or sent back), `app.emit_notification` patched with `pg_temp.patch`; signer columns classified `history` in `app.user_reference_columns()`. API `POST /changes/{id}/submit|send-to-board|recall`, `PUT …/impacts|board|cut-in|customer-approval`, `POST /change-signoffs/{id}/decide`, `GET /change-signers`, `GET /changes?signer=me`. UI: stepper, Impact assessment, Board and sign-off, Cut-in point, Customer approval cards, `/Change/:id/notice` (printable). Tests `change_assessment_approval.test.sql`, `change.e2e-spec.ts`, `Change.test.jsx`. **Not built (CH3+):** tasks, carrying out and closing, My changes, raise-from, the gate, dashboard, portal, default signers per function. **CH3 built (migration `20260432100000_change_tasks_closing.sql`, not yet applied live; pushing the API before it breaks the change detail, which selects the new tables and columns; plan §8c):** `change_tasks` (what, who (an active member), due date, impact area, open → done | cancelled, done → open; added once approved, until closed; **may point at the real record it changes** — part / control plan / document revision, gauge type, training session, supplier, inspection requirement, quality alert or risk — whose live status is read from it by `app.change_task_link_state`, never copied; a task on a revision is finished only once that revision is in force; one open task per change per record; finished by a manager **or the person it was given to**), `change_reviews` (the append-only effectiveness check: **effective closes the change, not effective sends it back to implementing**; closing needs the customer's approval decided when required and every linked revision still in force: `app.change_problems(change, 'close')`). **The stage after approval is worked out from the tasks** (`app.change_recompute_stage`: none live → approved, one open → implementing, all done → verifying; never set by hand; `app.change_rules` holds the moves); cancelling a change cancels its open tasks. Notices `change_tasks.created|updated` to the person a task is given to (`app.emit_notification` patched). `change_tasks.owner_user_id` is `history` in `app.user_reference_columns()`. API `POST /changes/{id}/tasks`, `PATCH|PUT /change-tasks/{id}[/link]`, `POST /change-tasks/{id}/complete|cancel|reopen|start-revision` (makes the revision **as the caller** in the same transaction, then links it), `POST /changes/{id}/reviews`, `GET /changes/mine[/count]`. UI `change/ChangeTasks.jsx` (Tasks and Effectiveness check cards), `MyChangesPage.jsx` (`/Change/Mine`, badge `my-changes`), stepper to Closed. Tests `change_tasks_closing.test.sql` (+ sweep seeds, guardrails list), `change.e2e-spec.ts`, `ChangeTasks.test.jsx`. **Not built:** screens to link the non-revision kinds, choosing an existing revision, task due-date reminders, the gate, dashboard, workflow triggers and portal (CH5/CH6), a journey step.
- **Quiet ticks (no migration; 2026-11-24)**: the worker tick used most of Vercel Hobby's Active CPU allowance (about 1,430 ticks a day, each 12 database round trips that found nothing). **pg_cron now calls it every five minutes** (`cron.alter_job` on `fortress-worker-tick`, `*/5 * * * *`; the runbook says the same) and **a tick that pg_cron starts runs the date-driven steps only on the first tick of each quarter of an hour** (`SLOW_STEPS` in `api/src/worker/worker.service.ts`: quality alerts, documents, reminders, scorecards, housekeeping, exchange rates, SSO domains; `slowStepsDue`, UTC minute % 15 < `WORKER_TICK_MINUTES`, default 5 — **set that variable if you change the schedule**); workflows, jobs, email, agent imports and imports run on every tick. A skipped step is not recorded in the tick. Ticks from any other source (`vercel`, `manual`, `test`) run everything, so the daily Vercel crons and every test are unchanged. Reminders can start up to 15 minutes after their time. Tests in `api/test/worker.e2e-spec.ts` ("quiet ticks"). Not built: a single "is anything due?" query to skip the event-driven steps too.
- **Reminders (WK2)** (`docs/workflow-engine-plan.md` §6; migration `20260220100000_reminders.sql`, applied live 2026-11-12; pushing the API before it breaks the organisation endpoints, which select the new columns, and the reminders routes): once a day at the organisation's **reminder time** (`organisation_settings.reminder_time`, default 07:00, in `time_zone` — null = the first active site's zone, `app.organisation_time_zone`; Settings → Organisation → Daily reminders, `ReminderTimeSetting.jsx`) the worker step `reminders` (`RemindersService.runDaily` → `app.system_run_reminders`, API login only, once per organisation and day via `app.reminder_runs`) tells each person what of theirs is overdue or due soon. **The rules live in one SQL function, `app.reminder_candidates(org, today)`**, by area (`app.reminder_areas()`: 8D actions, risk actions, risk reviews, management review actions, NC containment (owner employee's sign-in), audits (expected auditor in person), gauges (custodian), documents to read, quality alerts, periodic document reviews, SCAR responses, own competence expiring; each with its window); a new area needs a branch there, its key in `app.reminder_areas()`, `REMINDER_AREAS` + `reminderPath` (`api/src/reminders/reminder-paths.ts`) and `reminderPath` in `src/lib/reminders.js`. Each item is checked **as its person** (`app.reminder_item_allowed`: active member, area on, permission at the site) and the rows are written as the system: **`reminder_digests`** (one per person per day, never empty) and **`reminder_overdue_notices`** (once per record and due date, only for things that went overdue in the last three days), frozen, audited, readable by their person only, announced as notices addressed to the person with no actor (event group "Reminders", emailed by default). Per-area choices: `reminder_preferences` (`app.set_reminder_area`; `ReminderAreasCard.jsx` in Settings → Notifications). API `GET /reminders/mine`, `GET|PUT /reminder-areas`; org settings `reminderTime`, `timeZone`, `effectiveTimeZone`. UI `/Reminders` (`reminders/RemindersPage.jsx`, "My reminders" in the profile menu, where the digest notice leads). Tests `supabase/tests/reminders.test.sql`, `api/test/reminders.e2e-spec.ts`, `Reminders.test.jsx`, `SettingsNotifications.test.jsx`. **Not built:** supplier certificate expiry (no person to tell), a time per person, reminders before the window, escalation to managers (WF3).
- **Workflow rules (WF1)** (`docs/workflow-engine-plan.md` §6; migration `20260221100000_workflow_rules.sql`, applied live 2026-11-13, live guardrails 0 violations; pushing the API before it leaves the workflow routes failing): "when this happens, if these conditions hold, tell these people / give this person a task". Permissions `workflows.read` / `.manage`, errors `QM028` → `409 workflow_rule`. Triggers are outbox events (`app.workflow_triggers()` + `TRIGGERS` in `api/src/workflows/catalogue.ts`: NC raised / stage changed, inspection reading failed, calibration recorded, gauge status changed, 8D started, audit finding raised, supplier approval status changed); facts from `app.system_workflow_facts`; conditions evaluated in code (`conditions.ts`). `workflow_rules` (`WF-001`) + frozen `workflow_rule_versions` (a change to what a rule does needs a reason from version 2), `workflow_runs` (one per version and event), `workflow_notices` and `workflow_tasks` (`TSK-YY-NNNN`, `rule_name` snapshot) written only by API-login functions from the worker step `workflows` (consumer `workflows`, job `workflow.action`), once per run, action and person, only to members who may read the record at its site; notices own_record, no actor, emailed by default (`emit_notification` = the `20260220100000` version + these branches — **the latest definition**). Rules never change records. API `GET /workflow-triggers`, `GET|POST /workflow-rules`, `GET|PATCH /workflow-rules/{id}`, `POST …/status`, `GET /workflow-tasks/mine`, `POST /workflow-tasks/{id}/complete|reopen|cancel`. UI **My tasks** (`/Tasks`, second in the menu for everyone, badge `my-tasks`), home For you tile, notification links (`src/lib/workflows.js` mirrors `workflowRecordPath`). A new trigger needs: its row in `app.workflow_triggers()`, a branch in `app.system_workflow_facts`, its entry in `TRIGGERS`, and a record path in both `workflowRecordPath`s. The rule builder is WF2 (below).
- **Workflow rule builder (WF2)** (`docs/workflow-engine-plan.md` §6; migration `20260222100000_workflow_tests.sql`, applied live 2026-11-13; pushing the API before it leaves only the test routes failing): **Settings → Workflows** (`workflows.read`; writing and testing `workflows.manage`): `workflows/WorkflowsPage.jsx` (list) and `WorkflowRulePage.jsx` (`/Settings/Workflows/:id`, `new`): `RuleBuilder.jsx` (When / If all-or-any / Then as forms; record pickers for ref fields; a change to what the rule does asks for a reason and becomes a new version, a rename does not), Pause / Resume / Retire / Bring back, **What it did** (runs) and **Versions** in words, and **Test the rule** (`RuleTest.jsx`): the rule as on screen against one of the latest events (`GET /workflow-test-events?trigger`, `POST /workflow-tests`; `app.workflow_recent_events`, `app.workflow_preview_people`) — which conditions hold and whom each action would reach, with the same checks as the worker, **nothing saved or sent**. Rules carry `labels` (names for the ids they name). Helpers in `src/lib/workflows.js`, hooks in `useWorkflows.js`.
- **Workflow timers and lot hold (WF3)** (`docs/workflow-engine-plan.md` §6; migration `20260223100000_workflow_timers_lot_hold.sql`, applied live 2026-11-13): actions **Later, if still…** (`later`: after N days a `workflow.timer` job reads the record again via `app.system_workflow_timer_facts`, stops when the rule is off or the record gone, re-checks its own conditions and does up to five inner actions with the fresh facts at places `10 + 10 × index + j`) and **Hold the lot** (`hold_lot`: `app.system_workflow_hold_lot` holds the NC's / inspection's lot **as the person who saved the rule version**, who must still hold `production.manage` at the lot's site; refused → recorded and that person told). A hold is only allowed for triggers whose record names a lot (`LOT_ENTITIES` / `LOT_TRIGGERS`). `system_workflow_notify` / `_task` take the facts as an optional last argument. Scheduled supplier scorecards were built afterwards (SQ5b, below).
- **Workflow triggers and tests (WF4)** (`docs/workflow-engine-plan.md` §6; migration `20260225100000_workflow_more_triggers.sql`, applied live 2026-11-13; pushing the API before it leaves the new triggers never firing): **21 triggers** in `app.workflow_triggers()` / `TRIGGERS` (`api/src/workflows/catalogue.ts`) — the WF1 eight plus NC disposition approved (`ncr_disposition`), 8D verified / closed, audit completed (`audit_record`), supplier risk assessed, part revision released (`part_revision`), control plan revision status (`control_plan_revision`), and, from new AFTER triggers that only call `app.emit_event`: SPC alert raised (`spc_alert`), received-line decision (`receipt_line_disposition`), SCAR status (`scar`), scorecard band fell (`supplier_band_drop`), risk assessed (`risk_assessment`), quality alert issued (`quality_alert`). `app.system_workflow_facts` has a branch per entity type (all earlier branches kept, plus NC quantity / total cost / title, inspection work order / lot, calibration done in house or outside, 8D stage / title, finding title). **Condition tests:** field type `number` (`gt` `lt` `gte` `lte` `eq` `ne`, numeric) and `contains` / `not_contains` for text; `OPS_FOR` in `conditions.ts` says which tests fit which type (mirrored by `opsFor` in `src/lib/workflows.js`). Record paths for the new kinds in both `workflowRecordPath`s. The builder groups triggers by module (`triggerOptions`). `workflows.e2e-spec.ts` fails if the API catalogue and the database's triggers differ. A new trigger still needs: its row in `app.workflow_triggers()`, a branch in `app.system_workflow_facts`, its entry in `TRIGGERS`, and a record path in both `workflowRecordPath`s.
- **CMM and measurement file ingestion** (`docs/cmm-ingestion-plan.md`, decisions C1–C15 agreed 2026-11-14; slices CM0–CM6; **CM1 done**, migration `20260226100000_measurement_imports.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14 (6 tables with RLS, 4 audited, bucket and storage policies, no function callable by anon); pushing the API before it breaks the import routes): a CMM report file is uploaded on a running inspection, parsed and matched by the API, stored as a **preview** (`inspection_imports` `IMP-YY-NNNN`, `inspection_import_units` = parts in the file, `inspection_import_rows` = features), checked by a person and **applied** — the readings then go through the normal reading path, so they behave exactly like typed ones (pass/fail by the database, gauge state, sampling, SPC alerts, failures to acknowledge or raise; never an automatic NC). **Fortress's frozen limits decide pass/fail**; the database works out each row's expected result, a `problem` and a `mismatch` (file nominal/limits differ by more than 1/1000 of the tolerance band), and mismatches must be confirmed before applying. A header part number that is not the inspection's part is refused; a different work order or lot is a warning. Format **profiles** (`measurement_import_profiles` + frozen `_versions`, `process_control.manage`) say how a table export is read; **feature maps** (`measurement_feature_maps`, per part, CMM feature name → `characteristic_key`) remember matches. Applying = `app.begin_inspection_import_apply` (status `applying` + transaction setting `app.inspection_import`; refuses a preview over a day old, unconfirmed mismatches, nothing matched, the same file already applied (SHA-256), a correction without a reason / `inspection.correct`) → the API inserts samples and readings with `inspection_measurements.import_id` (refused at any other time and with any gauge but the import's) → `app.finish_inspection_import_apply` (every matched row became exactly that reading, by mode new / retest / correct); a deferred trigger refuses to commit an import left `applying`. Files in the private `inspection-files` bucket at `<org>/inspection/<inspection id>/…` (`app.inspection_file_allowed`). Functions only, `QM008` → `409 inspection_rule`, another organisation `P0002`. Tests `supabase/tests/measurement_imports.test.sql` (+ sweep, guardrails list). **CM2 done** (migration `20260227100000_measurement_import_units.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14; pushing the API before it breaks the import routes, which select `read_value`): `api/src/measurement-imports/` — `parse-report.ts` reads a table report with a profile's settings (`TableSettings` in `profile-settings.ts`: column headings, heading line fixed or found, header labels above the table, tolerances or limits, pass/fail words, unit, balloon from a column or the feature name, a column that splits the file into parts; ≤ 200 parts / 5,000 features / 25 MB), ready-made profiles CALYPSO table file, PC-DMIS CSV and a simple table (**column names to be checked against real exports**), `match.ts` (saved map first, then balloon; one feature per check per part); **units are converted by the database** (rows keep `read_*` + `unit_read`, `app.convert_measurement` fills `value_numeric`/`file_*` in the check's unit; incompatible units are a problem; a mismatch allows for the file's printed precision in another unit). Routes `/measurement-profiles` (+ `templates`, `try`, `{id}/retire|reactivate`), `/parts/{id}/feature-maps`, `/measurement-feature-maps/{id}/end`, `POST|GET /inspections/{id}/imports`, `/inspection-imports/{id}` (+ `confirm-mismatches`, `discard`), `PUT /inspection-import-rows/{id}/match {requirementId, remember}`; files read with the service key via `InspectionFileStore` (tests use a memory store). Tests `parse-report.spec.ts`, `match.spec.ts`, `measurement_import_units.test.sql`, `measurement-imports.e2e-spec.ts` (+ API sweep). **CM3 done** (no migration): `POST /inspection-imports/{id}/apply {mode new|retest|correct, reason?, gaugeId?, gaugeOverrideReason?, newSubgroup?, triggerType?}` — one transaction: begin → each part recorded through **`InspectionService.recordSample`** (split out of `addSample`; sampling plan, database pass/fail, gauge override, SPC alerts — a file cannot add a sample the plan does not ask for; `triggerType` gives an extra one's reason) or, for a retest, re-measuring each check's latest failed reading, or, for a correction (reason + `inspection.correct`), replacing the current readings of the part's sample (found by the file's unit reference) → finish; values passed as exact text. Readings carry `importId` in the inspection detail. **CM4 done** (screens, no migration): **Measurement files** card on the inspection page and job hub (`measurement-imports/ImportFromFile.jsx`: files read for the inspection; **Import from CMM** → dialog with profile, gauge, drop zone; uploads to `inspection-files`, opens the preview; profile and gauge remembered per machine in local storage), **preview page** `/Inspections/:inspectionId/imports/:importId` (`ImportPreviewPage.jsx`, `?back=` returns to the job: figures, header, warnings, confirm the differences, per part the features with an optimistic check combo (`useMatchFeature`, remember for the part), values in the check's unit, both limits, expected result or problem; **Record N readings** with New / Retest / Correction, gauge, why the sample was taken, override reason; Discard), "from a file" on readings, **Settings → Data Management → Process control → Measurement files** (`MeasurementProfilesCard.jsx`: profiles from a ready-made one, the column / reading / header editor, **Try a file**, reason for a new version, retire / bring back) and **CMM feature matches** on the part page (`FeatureMapsCard.jsx`). Hooks `useMeasurementImports.js`, helpers `lib/measurementImports.js`, tests `MeasurementImports.test.jsx`. **CM5 done** (QIF Results, no migration): `parse-qif.ts` (own small XML reader, **a DOCTYPE is refused**; characteristics followed measurement → item (designator = balloon) → nominal → definition (tolerances), units from `FileUnits`, a part per `MeasurementResults` with its serial) gives the same `ParsedReport`, so a `.qif` / `.xml` file is read **without a profile** (`profileId` optional; format `qif`; a table file without one is `400 profile_required`); the upload dialog takes QIF files and hides the profile choice for them. **PC-DMIS generic exports** (File › Export › Generic: no heading row, `LABEL AX:X, nominal, +tol, -tol, measured, deviation, out of tol`) are recognised by their lines and read without a profile too (`parse-pcdmis-generic.ts`: lower = nominal − minus tolerance as written, feature/hits lines skipped, no unit = the check's, a single-axis label tried as a balloon, a repeated dimension starts the next part; format `table`, no profile, an information warning); `createImport` tries QIF → generic → the profile, and the dialog's profile is optional. Plan §13. **CM6 done (exit check, `docs/cmm-ingestion-exit-report.md`, no migration):** live guardrails 0 violations, advisors nothing new, and **`npm run journey` has a CMM step** (`cmmImports()` in `scripts/journey/journey.cjs`; the stack serves the `inspection-files` bucket to the API's `InspectionFileStore`): a PC-DMIS generic export read on the job hub with no profile, one feature matched by hand and remembered, three readings recorded, the job completed; on the next job a QIF file matched by balloons and the remembered match, one reading out of tolerance. The browser found that how a file was read showed as a warning: readers now return `notes`, stored as warnings of kind `info` and drawn as information. The CMM ingestion plan is complete; real sample files (CM0) are still needed to confirm the ready-made profiles, the QIF element names and the PC-DMIS generic details.
- **Organisation security** (`docs/security-plan.md`, decisions S1–S12 agreed; slices SE0–SX). **SE0 done** (Vercel overwrites `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-IP`, `X-Vercel-Forwarded-For` and `Forwarded` with the caller's real address, so `req.ip` with `trust proxy` = 1 (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, default in production) cannot be forged; `clientAddress()` in `api/src/security/client-address.ts` normalises it; `RequestContext.address`). **SE1 done** (migration `20260228100000_organisation_security.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14, live guardrails 0 violations; pushing the API before it breaks every organisation route, because the guard calls `app.network_allowed`): permissions `security.read` / `.manage` (owner, admin); `organisation_settings.network_policy` off | monitor | enforce (only `app.set_network_policy`; enforce needs an active network containing the caller's address); `organisation_networks` (Postgres `cidr`, normalised by `app.parse_network`: single address → /32 or /128, IPv4-in-IPv6 → IPv4, host bits refused with the network meant, private / special / wider than /8 or /16 refused; name and range unique among live; removed with a reason, restored switched off; never deleted; functions only, audited); **lockout rule**: while enforced a change that leaves the caller's address outside every active network is refused (`QM029` → `409 security_rule`); `app.network_allowed(org, ip)` → open | allowed | monitored | denied (NULL for non-members); `access_denials` (per organisation, person, address, mode, hour; API login writes, `security.read` reads, 90-day purge). **SE2 done** (API, no migration): `OrganisationGuard` checks the network in its one query right after membership — `denied` → `403 network_not_allowed` `{ address }`, denied and monitored requests recorded on the API login; routes `GET|PUT /security/access-policy` (enforce needs `confirm: true`), `POST /security/networks`, `PATCH /security/networks/{id}`, `POST …/remove|restore`, `GET /security/access-denials`; `/me` memberships carry `network { state, address }`; worker step `housekeeping` purges old denials; the SE0 diagnostic route is gone. Tests `organisation_security.test.sql`, `security.e2e-spec.ts`, `client-address.spec.ts`. **SE3 done** (migration `20260301100000_storage_through_api.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14): **organisation files go through the API** — `app.storage_file_allowed(bucket, name, write)` is the one rule per organisation bucket (the storage policies and the API both call it) and `app.storage_direct_allowed(name)` refuses direct storage access while the organisation enforces, except for the API's own login (`session_user = 'qms_api'`); `POST /files/upload-url|download-url|remove` (`api/src/files/`, `FileLinkSigner` with the service key, `503 file_storage_unavailable` without it; download links default 10 minutes, at most an hour, five minutes while enforced); the browser helpers in `src/api/supabaseApi.js` (`uploadFileToSupabase`, `uploadToPath`, `generateSignedUrl`, `removeOrganisationFile`) ask the API and upload with `uploadToSignedUrl`; **nothing but the helper and the avatar code may call `supabase.storage.from`** (`supabaseApi.test.js` fails otherwise); avatars stay the person's own. Tests `storage_through_api.test.sql`, `files.e2e-spec.ts`. **SE4 done** (migration `20260302100000_network_restricted_emails.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14): **Settings → Security** (`Settings/SettingsSecurity.jsx`, `security.read`; hooks `useSecurity.js`, words `lib/security.js`) holds the two-factor switch (moved from Organisation), the Off / Monitor / Enforce choice (enforce asks for the typed word "enforce" and says who would be blocked), the caller's address with **Add this address**, the networks (optimistic on/off, edit, remove with a reason, bring back) and recent blocked attempts; **`NetworkBlocked.jsx`** replaces the whole app when `/me` says the organisation denies this network or any request answers `403 network_not_allowed` (`apiFetch` raises the `qms:network-blocked` event, `App.jsx` listens); the switcher marks such organisations; while enforced, notification emails say only "You have a new notification in Fortress — <organisation>" and link (`app.system_lease_emails` returns `network_restricted`). **SE5 done** (migration `20260303100000_network_policy_recovery.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14): every change of the policy is written to `network_policy_changes` by a trigger (frozen, audited, `security.read`) and told to owners and admins (event `network_policy_changes.created`, **no actor** so the enforcer is told too, emailed in full even while enforced, with what to do if everyone is locked out; `app.emit_notification` = the `20260221100000` version + this table in the no-actor list — **the latest definition**); **recovery**: `app.platform_recover_network_policy(org, reason, verification)` (platform operator with MFA; only while enforced; back to monitor, never off; networks kept) → `POST /platform/organisations/{id}/recover-network-policy`, **Recover access** on the operator panel's organisations tab (which shows each policy); Settings → Security lists the changes. **SE6 done** (migration `20260304100000_security_who_rules.sql`, applied live 2026-11-14; pushing the API before it breaks every organisation route, because the guard calls `app.max_session_hours`): **allowed email domains** (`organisation_settings.allowed_email_domains`, `app.set_allowed_email_domains`; an entry allows its sub-domains; trigger `organisation_users_email_domain` refuses an invitation outside them, `QM015` → `409 invitation_rule`; members already outside are listed (`app.members_outside_email_domains`), never removed) and **maximum session age** (`organisation_settings.max_session_hours` 1–720, `app.set_max_session_hours`; `OrganisationGuard` takes the sign-in time from the token's `amr` claim (`signedInAt` / `sessionTooOld`) and answers `401 reauthentication_required`; `apiFetch` raises `qms:reauthentication-required`, `App.jsx` signs out and the sign-in page says why); `PUT /security/email-domains|session-age`; cards **Who can be invited** and **Sign in again after** on Settings → Security. **SX done (exit check, `docs/security-exit-report.md`, no migration):** live guardrails 0 violations, advisors nothing new, the proxy set-up per environment written down (Vercel: `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` unset = 1; local/tests 0; a CDN in front needs 2 and a locked origin), and **`npm run journey` has a security step** (`networkRestrictions()` in `scripts/journey/journey.cjs`; the stack runs the API with `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS=1` and Playwright sets `X-Forwarded-For` on the browser's API requests: the owner at 203.0.113.10 approves the address, monitors, sees Colin's request from 198.51.100.200, enforces; Colin gets the blocked screen; an operator recovers to monitor; the owner switches it off). The organisation security plan is complete.
- **Fortress Connector Agent** (`docs/connector-agent-plan.md`, from `Fortress_Connector_Agent_Design_Brief.md`; decisions AG-D1–AG-D12 agreed 2026-11-15; slices AG0–AG6): a Windows service (.NET 10 LTS, C#) the customer installs in their network that connects **outwards only** to the API, takes approved jobs (test, discover, preview, extract) and uploads rows in batches, which feed the **existing** import pipeline (stored like file chunks, read by an `AgentAdapter` in the sync engine; templates, mapping, preview, ownership, missing records and health unchanged). Code lives in `connector-agent/` (outside the npm workspaces; `bin/` and `obj/` ignored). Agreed: requests signed with the agent's own key (no mTLS or WebSockets on Vercel; polling), database passwords sealed in the browser to the agent's key and kept only on the agent (DPAPI) or Windows authentication, no SQL from the cloud (a structured spec the agent turns into a parameterised, read-only SELECT, plus an optional local allow-list), batches ≤ 1,000 rows gzipped, typed values, incremental by a "changed since" column with the existing cursor, schedules every 5 / 15 / 60 minutes, agent requests checked against the network policy, managing agents needs `integrations.manage` (a Controller seat). **AG0 done (spike, `connector-agent/spike/run.sh`):** Ed25519 is not in .NET 10, so signatures are **ECDSA P-256 / SHA-256** (built into .NET, Node and browsers); sealed credentials are ECDH P-256 → HKDF-SHA256 → AES-256-GCM with the connection id as associated data; 1,000 × 20-column rows = 428 kB JSON / 58 kB gzip, and **the batch route must read the raw gzip and inflate it itself with a cap** (the JSON parser's limit applies after inflating); extraction proven against a real PostgreSQL with a SELECT-only login (keyset paging in a read-only transaction, unknown identifiers refused, writes refused `25006`); **discovery must read the system catalogue** (`information_schema` hides keys from a SELECT-only login). The .NET SDK is installed in `~/.dotnet` in this container; there is no SQL Server here (Docker does not work), so the SQL Server adapter needs a Windows machine with SQL Server (AG-D11). **AG1 done (plan §14; migration `20260305100000_connector_agents.sql`, applied live 2026-11-15 (6 tables with RLS, 3 audited, 7 agent functions API-login only, live guardrails 0 violations)):** tables `integration_agents`, `integration_agent_registrations` (sha256 of a one-time `FAGT-…` token, 1–168 h, `replaces_agent_id` for a reinstall), `integration_agent_keys` (signing + sealing P-256 SPKI, one active), `integration_agent_nonces` (purged by housekeeping), `integration_agent_jobs` (`ping` now; pending → received → running → completed | failed | cancelled, lease 5 min, retried while attempts remain), `integration_agent_events` (frozen); functions only, people's need `integrations.manage`, `app.system_agent_*` API login only; agent requests checked against the network policy (`403 network_not_allowed`, denial recorded with no person). API `api/src/integrations/agents/`: **`AgentGuard`** verifies `FORTRESS-AGENT-V1\nMETHOD\npath?query\nsha256(body)\nts\nnonce\nagentId` (ECDSA P-256 / SHA-256, 64-byte r‖s; ±300 s; nonce once; codes `agent_unauthenticated|agent_clock|agent_revoked|agent_replay`); people `GET /integrations/agents`, `GET|PATCH …/{id}`, `POST …/{id}/revoke|wake|ping`, `GET /integrations/agent-jobs/{id}`, `GET|POST /integrations/agent-registrations` (token shown once, `apiUrl` from `AGENT_API_URL` else the request host, `downloadUrl` from `AGENT_DOWNLOAD_URL`), `POST …/{id}/cancel`; agent (`@Public`, signed) `POST /integrations/agent/register` (subject `register`, signed with the new key) `|heartbeat|jobs/next|jobs/{id}/ack|progress|complete|fail|keys/rotate|deregister`; online = heartbeat within 180 s; poll 60 s, 15 s while jobs wait or the page is open (wake). UI `src/components/integrations/agents/` (`/Settings/Integrations/Agents[/:id]`: list, Add with the token once, agent page with **Check now**, history, Rename / Reinstall / Revoke), card on Settings → Integrations, hooks `useAgents.js`, `lib/agents.js`. **Agent** `connector-agent/` (.NET 10: `Fortress.Agent.Core`, `Fortress.Agent.Host` = `FortressAgent.exe register|run|status|deregister` + Windows service `FortressConnectorAgent`, xUnit `tests/`, `install/install.ps1|uninstall.ps1`, `package.sh` → self-contained win-x64 zip, `run-e2e.sh` → `api/test/connector-agent-binary.e2e-spec.ts` runs the real agent against the real API: register, online, ping answered, exit 0 on revoke). **`connector-agent/contract/`** (signature vector + message bodies) is checked by both `agent-signature.spec.ts` and the agent's `ContractTests` — change a message there first. Tests `connector_agents.test.sql`, `connector-agents.e2e-spec.ts` (+ API sweep), `ConnectorAgents.test.jsx`. **Not run on Windows yet** (service, DPAPI, install scripts): the acceptance check on the Windows machine. **AG2 done (plan §15; migration `20260306100000_agent_database_connections.sql`, applied live 2026-11-15 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API before it breaks every connection endpoint, which selects `agent_id`):** provider `sql_agent` available; `integration_connections.agent_id` (required for it, fixed); settings `engine` sqlserver|postgres, `server`, `port` or `instance`, `database`, `authType` windows|sql|password, `encrypt`, `trustServerCertificate`, `timeoutSeconds` (`app.integration_agent_config_problem`; nothing that could change a connection string). **The database password is sealed in the browser** (`src/lib/sealCredential.js`, WebCrypto ECDH P-256 → HKDF → AES-GCM, connection id as AAD) to the agent's current key and delivered as a `store_credential` job (`app.set_agent_credential`); Fortress keeps only `integration_agent_credentials` (waiting|stored|failed|cleared) and **the sealed bytes leave the job when it ends** (trigger). Jobs for a connection (`app.request_connection_job`): `test_connection` (answer recorded on the connection as of when asked), `discover` (schemas | objects | columns), `preview` (≤ 50 rows) — structured, never SQL. Activation also needs the agent live and the password stored; reinstall / revoke clear passwords and recheck. `app.integration_activation_problems_internal` (no membership check) is what `app.integration_recheck` uses. (The AG2 import guard was removed in AG3.) API: `POST /integrations/connections` takes `agentId`, detail carries `agent` (`sealingKey`, `keyId`), `agentCredential`, `agentJobs`; `PUT …/{id}/agent-credential`, `POST …/{id}/agent-jobs`, `POST …/{id}/test` asks the agent. Agent: `Fortress.Agent.Adapters` (PostgreSQL, SQL Server; read-only transactions, catalogue with the login's rights, the one data query built from catalogue names, quoted, row count as a parameter), `CredentialStore` (DPAPI file per connection), **`allowlist.json`** on the agent's machine (`databases`/`schemas`/`objects`, `*`), handlers store_credential/test_connection/discover/preview, **2-second job polling while someone has a page open**. UI: PostgreSQL and SQL Server cards → `integrations/database/DatabaseConnectionDialog.jsx`; connection page `AgentCard` (password typed and sealed), `BrowseCard` (schemas → tables → columns → preview), agent jobs. Tests `agent_database_connections.test.sql`, `agent-connections.e2e-spec.ts`, agent xUnit (incl. `PostgresAdapterTests` against a real PostgreSQL), `connector-agent-binary.e2e-spec.ts` (real agent: password, test, browse, preview), `DatabaseConnections.test.jsx`, `sealCredential.test.js`; contract `sealed-credential.json`. `scripts/db-check.sh`: `PGLISTEN_CHECK=127.0.0.1` adds TCP, and the cleanup now stops the server as `postgres` (a failed run as root used to leave it running). **SQL Server has not run against a real server yet** (none here): the Windows machine is the check. **AG3 done (plan §16; migration `20260307100000_agent_extraction.sql`, applied live 2026-11-15 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API before it breaks every connection endpoint (it selects `integration_record_sources`) and the runs endpoints (they join `integration_agent_jobs.run_id`)):** **record sources** `integration_record_sources` (per connection and kind of record: `{schema, object, keyColumn, columns?, filters?}` with filters `column op value`, ops `eq ne gt gte lt lte is_null not_null`; `app.set_integration_record_source(connection, entity, source|null)`, `integrations.manage`; does **not** reset the connection's approval) → `PUT /integrations/connections/{id}/record-source {entityType, source|null}`, detail `recordSources`. A preview / full run of a database connection needs a source and comes with an **`extract` job** (`integration_agent_jobs.run_id`, preview ≤ 1,000 rows, incremental refused until AG4); the run **waits for the agent** (lease answers `waitingForAgent`, drain `waiting`, the worker skips it) and is due as soon as the job completes; a failed / cancelled extraction fails the run, a stopped run cancels its extraction. Batches: agent `POST /integrations/agent/jobs/{id}/batches` = gzip of `{seq, lastKey, rows}` (`Content-Type: application/gzip`, raw body parser 1 MB, inflated with an 8 MB cap → `413 payload_too_large`, `X-Batch-Checksum` = SHA-256 of the JSON inside → `400 batch_checksum`, `agent-batch.ts`) stored once and in order in `integration_agent_batches` (**never readable by people**: SELECT policy `false`; `app.system_agent_store_batch`; completing an extraction needs every batch; a job taken again gets `resume {nextSeq, afterKey, rows}`; deleted 7 days after the job ends). `AgentAdapter` (`providers/sql-agent/`) pages through the run's batches like file chunks (values as text). A database connection's **sample** = its latest completed preview job of the source's table (`409 agent_sample_needed` otherwise), so templates and `templates/preview` work unchanged. Runs carry `agent {status, rows, batches, errorMessage}`. Agent: `ExtractHandler` (`Extraction.cs`: keyset `ExtractPageAsync` in both adapters, every value a parameter, batches ≤ 1,000 rows halved while over 900 kB gzip, text ≤ 5,000, `DbValues.KeyText`, resume, stops when cancelled). UI: **Records read from the database** card and **Use this table** → `RecordSourceDialog` (`integrations/database/RecordSources.jsx`), `ApiMappingPage` maps database connections too (asks the agent for a sample), Run dialog by sources, run page shows the agent's progress (2-second polling), shared `database/agentAsk.jsx`. Tests `agent_extraction.test.sql`, `agent-batch.spec.ts`, `agent-extraction.e2e-spec.ts` (helpers `api/test/agent-helpers.ts`), `connector-agent-binary.e2e-spec.ts` (real agent imports 1,100 PostgreSQL rows in two batches, again with nothing written), `ExtractionTests.cs`, `PostgresAdapterTests.cs`, `AgentImports.test.jsx`. **AG4 done (plan §17; migration `20260308100000_agent_sync.sql`, applied live 2026-11-15 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API before it breaks agent batches, the new schedules and the worker's `agent-imports` step):** a record source may name **`changedColumn`** (must be a read column); an incremental database run's `extract` job carries `changedSince` = the saved mark (`{"w": …}` in `integration_sync_cursors`), the agent filters `changed >= since`, keeps the highest value read (`DbValues.HigherMark`) and sends it as `changedUpTo` with every batch (kept for resume) and in its result; `AgentAdapter`'s last page returns `{"w": changedUpTo ?? changedSince}` (a full read sets the mark too; an incremental run's cursor is the mark, so the adapter starts at batch 0). **Schedules** `manual|every_5m|every_15m|hourly|daily` (minute ones only for `sql_agent`; need the source and a template); worker step **`agent-imports`** = `app.system_fail_stalled_agent_jobs()` (jobs over 15 minutes old for an agent unheard for 15 minutes fail with "did not answer") + `app.system_schedule_integration_runs(current_date, false)` (never beside a waiting/running run or while the agent is offline; incremental when a changed column and a mark exist). `app.integration_health` carries `agent`; disappeared rows are found after a full read as for other connections. UI: changed-column combo in the source dialog, Incremental in the Run dialog for such a table, **Schedule** card on database connections (`ScheduleCard`, `scheduleOptions`/`scheduleLabel` in `lib/integrations.js`), health shows the agent offline. Tests `agent_sync.test.sql`, `agent-batch.spec.ts`, `agent-extraction.e2e-spec.ts`, `connector-agent-binary.e2e-spec.ts` (real agent: incremental read of real PostgreSQL updates only the changed rows), agent `ExtractionTests.cs`/`PostgresAdapterTests.cs`, `AgentImports.test.jsx`. **AG5a done (plan §18; migration `20260309100000_agent_mysql.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16; only `app.integration_agent_config_problem` changes, so pushing the API first only refuses MySQL connections):** engine **`mysql`** (MySQL / MariaDB, password sign-in, port 3306, no instance). Agent `MySqlAdapter` (MySqlConnector 3.0.0): `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY` per call, catalogue from `information_schema`, backtick quoting, values as parameters, **the connection's database is the one schema** (others refused), `AllowLoadLocalInfile=false`, zero dates as text. UI: MySQL card available, form without instance. Tests `agent_mysql.test.sql`, `MySqlAdapterTests.cs` against a real MariaDB from **`connector-agent/mysql-check.sh`** (run by `run-e2e.sh` when MariaDB/MySQL is installed; `apt-get install mariadb-server` works here), binary e2e (the same agent imports from MySQL while its PostgreSQL connection stays in use), `DatabaseConnections.test.jsx`. Not yet checked against MySQL 8. **AG5b done (plan §18; migration `20260310100000_agent_folders.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API first only leaves the folder routes failing):** engine **`folder`** of the agent connection (`sql_agent`, now "Database or folder through a connector agent"): `folder` path (drive, share or absolute; `app.integration_folder_config_problem`), no sign-in, file reading options as for uploads. **The agent reads only folders listed under `"folders"` in its `allowlist.json` (none = no folder)** and only top-level files; a record source is `{file}` = name or pattern, the **newest** matching CSV/TXT/TSV/XLSX is read. **The agent never parses:** it sends the bytes in signed parts (`POST /integrations/agent/jobs/{id}/file-parts`, `X-Part-Seq`, `X-Part-Checksum`, ≤ 1 MB each, ≤ 25 MB; `integration_agent_file_parts`, never readable, cleared when the job ends) and on `complete` the API (`AgentsService.complete`, `agent-file.ts`) checks size and SHA-256, reads it with `parseFile` and stores a preview's first rows or an import's rows as batches. Extract jobs carry `changedColumn: '@file'`; the mark is the file's SHA-256, so an incremental read sends nothing for an unchanged file. UI: **Files in a folder** card, folder dialog, folder browse card (`FolderBrowseCard`), `FileSourceDialog` (`filePatternFrom`), folder words. Tests `agent_folders.test.sql`, `FolderTests.cs`, `agent-file.spec.ts`, `agent-folders.e2e-spec.ts`, binary e2e (real folder), `AgentImports.test.jsx`, `DatabaseConnections.test.jsx`. **AG5c done (plan §18; migration `20260311100000_agent_rest.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API first only refuses internal API connections):** engine **`rest`** of the agent connection (provider now "Database, folder or API through a connector agent"): `baseUrl` http/https on the customer's network (private addresses fine; link-local / "any" refused; `app.integration_agent_rest_config_problem`), `authType` none / api_key (+ `apiKeyHeader`) / bearer / basic, `testPath`, `timeoutSeconds`. **The agent calls only hosts listed under `"hosts"` in its `allowlist.json` (none = no API)**, never follows redirects, checks every resolved address (`RestHttp.Refused`) and keeps next-page links on the same address. The key/token is sealed in the browser like a password (**empty user name**; `CredentialStore` accepts it). A record source is an **endpoint** `{path, recordsPath?, query?, paging?, keyColumn, changedColumn?, changedParam?}` (`app.integration_rest_source_problem`; the cloud connector's paging); incremental reads send `changedParam` = the highest `changedColumn` read (compared as the cloud connector does). **The agent flattens records exactly as the cloud REST connector** (`JsNode` / `RestFlatten` in `Rest.cs`; JavaScript key order, number printing and `JSON.stringify` escaping); `connector-agent/contract/rest-flatten.json` is generated from `flattenRecord` and checked by `rest-flatten.spec.ts` and `RestTests`. Rows go as the extraction's batches (lastKey = the page to ask next + records already sent, for resume). API: `RecordSource` takes the endpoint shape, agent-job requests take `path`/`recordsPath`/`query`/`paging`, a sample = the latest preview of the same path and list. UI: **Internal API** card, API fields in the connection dialog, one-field token / API key dialog, `RestSources.jsx` (`RestTryCard` "Try an address", `RestSourceDialog`), API words on the sources card, mapping page, Run dialog and schedule. Tests `agent_rest.test.sql`, `RestTests.cs`, `rest-flatten.spec.ts`, `agent-rest.e2e-spec.ts`, binary e2e (real HTTP API on 127.0.0.1), `DatabaseConnections.test.jsx`, `AgentImports.test.jsx`. **AG6 (plan §19, decisions AG6-D1–D5) is in four slices: AG6a resilience, AG6b updates, AG6c installer and signing (Windows), AG6d exit check.** **AG6a built (migration `20260312100000_agent_resilience.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API first only leaves rotate-keys / diagnostics failing):** job kinds `rotate_keys` and `diagnostics` (agent-level, no connection; one waiting rotation at a time), `POST /integrations/agents/{id}/rotate-keys|diagnostics` (`integrations.manage`), **Keys and diagnostics** card on the agent page (bundle in words, download). The agent rotates its keys every 90 days on its own (`KeyRotation` in `Maintenance.cs`: new keys written as pending first, recovered if Fortress already has them; previous sealing key kept 30 days and tried by `store_credential`); `DiagnosticsHandler` sends ≤ 64 kB, redacted; the worker's housekeeping clears bundles after 30 days (`app.system_purge_agent_diagnostics`). **A running job's calls to Fortress are retried for about three minutes (`AgentRunner.PatientlyAsync`) and a dropped database connection is a passing failure** (the extraction is tried again and resumes). The binary e2e has **recovery cases** (agent killed, API unreachable through a switchable proxy, database connection ended) and an opt-in **load test** (`AGENT_LOAD_ROWS`, `AGENT_LOAD_REPORT`). **AG6b built (migration `20260313100000_agent_updates.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16 (live guardrails 0 violations); pushing the API first breaks the agent page, which selects the new tables):** signed releases and approved updates. A release = a manifest text (`fortress-agent-release-v1`: version, platform win-x64|linux-x64, https url, sha256, size, notes) signed with ECDSA P-256 by a release key (`connector-agent/release/make-release-key.mjs`, `sign-release.mjs`; **the private key never enters the repository**). An operator publishes it on `/platform` → **Agent releases** (`POST /platform/agent-releases`, checked against env **`AGENT_RELEASE_KEYS`**, `503 release_keys_unavailable` without it; withdraw with a reason cancels waiting updates); `integration_agent_releases` is global and frozen. Someone with `integrations.manage` approves it per agent on the agent page's **Updates** card (`POST /integrations/agents/{id}/updates`, `…/agent-updates/{id}/cancel` while waiting; one open update per agent, platform must match, newer only) → an `update_agent` job; `integration_agent_updates` waiting → staged (job done) → applied (heartbeat at the new version) | failed | rolled_back | cancelled. **The agent checks the signature again against `ReleaseKeys.Built`** (release key 1, key id `bbbe9b808fda7ca1`, built in 2026-11-16; the private half is kept by the owner, never in the repository; `AGENT_RELEASE_KEYS` on the API must hold the same public key; `UpdateTests` checks every built-in key is a P-256 SPKI), downloads, checks size and SHA-256, unpacks safely and stages under `updates/`; on Windows `FortressAgent.exe apply-update` swaps the service folder and puts the old one back if the new one does not check in within 5 minutes (only tested with a fake service so far). Test key / download override only in Debug or `-p:FortressTestHooks=true` builds (`run-e2e.sh`), never `package.sh`. Tests `agent_updates.test.sql`, `agent-updates.e2e-spec.ts`, `agent-release.spec.ts`, `UpdateTests.cs`, binary e2e update step, `ConnectorAgents.test.jsx`, `PlatformPanels.test.jsx`. **AG6d security review done (plan §19 "AG6d security review"; migration `20260314100000_agent_hardening.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16, live guardrails 0 violations):** **a stored password is bound to where it was typed for** (`ConnectionTarget`: engine, server, port, instance, database, sign-in kind, API address and key header; the agent refuses it elsewhere with `CredentialStore.TargetChanged`; `store_credential` jobs now carry `connection`) and the database **clears it when the target changes and refuses an engine change** (`app.integration_agent_target_rules`, `QM022`); allow-list **`"servers"`** (Windows sign-in only to listed servers, none = none; once listed, every sign-in is limited to them) and **`"plainHttpSignIn": true`** (otherwise a key or password is never sent to an `http://` internal API); a folder reached through a **link below the allowed folder** is refused and link files are never listed or read; PostgreSQL refuses cleartext password auth (`RequireAuth`); `.xlsx` unpack limits (150 MB total, 120 MB a part, 5,000 entries) in `file-parsers.ts`; `AgentGuard` refuses an address after 30 failed signatures a minute (`429 agent_too_many_failures`) and registration is rate-limited again. **The Windows swap is done by a second service, `FortressConnectorAgentUpdater`** (LocalSystem, on demand, its own copy in `Program Files\Fortress\ConnectorAgentUpdater`; the agent's account may only start it, service SDDL `(A;;RPLCLOCRRC;;;<agent SID>)`): `UpdatePreparer` checks the signature, platform and version again with its own keys, copies the package to `<install>.staging`, checks size and SHA-256 there and unpacks that copy; exit codes applied 0, rolled back 4, refused 5. **AG6c done (passing in CI, run 8, 2026-11-16: 41 Windows checks incl. the update swap and roll-back, and the agent tests against SQL Server 2022, MySQL 8, PostgreSQL 16):** WiX 5 MSI (`connector-agent/installer/`: both services, data folder and its parent owned by Administrators with only SYSTEM/Administrators/service, `allowlist.json` from `install/allowlist.json` read-only for the service and never overwritten, `API_URL` + hidden `TOKEN`, removal deregisters and clears update leftovers); `install.ps1`/`uninstall.ps1` do the same and refuse a data folder owned by someone else; `connector-agent/release/sign-windows.ps1` (signtool + RFC 3161 timestamp; thumbprint or `FORTRESS_SIGN_PFX_BASE64`/`_PASSWORD` secrets; `-Required` for a release); **`.github/workflows/connector-agent.yml`**: `databases` (agent tests against SQL Server 2022, MySQL 8, PostgreSQL 16 containers; `SqlServerAdapterTests.cs` gated on `AGENT_TEST_SQLSERVER`) and `windows` (release + test MSI, `connector-agent/ci/windows-checks.ps1` against `ci/fake-fortress.mjs`: install, services, ACLs, registration, removal, an update swapped in and one rolled back, then the agent tests on Windows). **Journey step `connectorAgent()`** (`scripts/journey/fake-agent.cjs`: the real protocol against the real API): add an agent, register with the shown token, Online, Check now, a PostgreSQL connection whose password is sealed in the browser and opened by the agent exactly as typed, revoke. Exit report `docs/connector-agent-exit-report.md`. **The load test found that a large import's run page timed out** (RLS checked `integrations.read` per inbox row, and the `run_id or last_run_id` count had no index): migration `20260315100000_integration_run_rows_fast.sql` (applied live 2026-11-16, before the API was pushed; pushing the API without it breaks every run's page) adds `app.integration_run_row_counts(run)` / `app.integration_run_rows(run, status, limit, offset)` (one permission check, indexed reads, ≤ 500 a page) and an index on `integration_inbox (last_run_id, status)`; the run detail and rows endpoints use them. A new count over a run's inbox rows should use these, not a query under RLS.
- **Single sign-on (SAML)** (`docs/sso-plan.md`, decisions SS1–SS12 agreed 2026-11-16; slices SS0–SSX). **SS0 done without the paid plan** (plan §12): the Supabase organisation is on the **free plan** and SAML needs **Pro**. A scoped personal access token (one project, **Auth Config: Read-write**) can manage SSO providers. From the Auth server's source: a SAML session is **`aal1`**; `amr` lists methods most recent first, and a SAML entry carries the provider uuid, so check the whole array; one domain cannot be on two providers; an unknown domain is `404 sso_provider_not_found`; SSO users are never linked to password users with the same email. **SS1 done** (migration `20260316100000_sso_domains.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16; plan §13): SSO settings on `organisation_settings` (`sso_mode` off/optional/required, `sso_join`, default role/department, `sso_trust_idp_mfa`; nothing writes them until SS3) and `organisation_sso_domains`. A domain is proved by the TXT record `fortress-verification=<token>`; the token is made by the database. Only the API login records a check (`app.system_sso_domain_check`). One organisation holds a verified domain at a time, it must agree with the allowed email domains, and it is removed with a reason. `QM030` → `409 sso_rule`. API `GET /security/sso`, `POST /security/sso/domains`, `…/{id}/verify|remove` (`api/src/sso/`, `DnsTxtResolver`); worker step `sso-domains` re-checks weekly. UI: **Single sign-on** card on Settings → Security (`Settings/SingleSignOn.jsx`); `ui/CopyButton.jsx` is now shared. **SS2 done** (migration `20260317100000_sso_providers.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16; plan §14): `organisation_sso_providers` (one active per organisation, disabled kept; written only by the API login's `app.system_sso_*`), `app.handle_new_user` reads `given_name` / `family_name` / `name` from a company sign-in, and `app.accept_terms(version)`. API `SupabaseSsoAdmin` (Management API with **`SUPABASE_MANAGEMENT_TOKEN`**, a token scoped to the project and Auth Config: Read-write; unset → `503 sso_unavailable`), `PUT /security/sso/provider {metadataUrl|metadataXml}`, `POST …/provider/disable {reason}`, domains synced to Supabase on verify / remove, `/me` `termsAcceptedAt` + `POST /me/terms`. UI: provider steps on the card (`Settings/SsoProvider.jsx`), **Sign in with your company account** → `/sso` (`auth/SsoSignIn.jsx`, `?domain=` / `?provider=`), `/auth/callback`, `auth/FirstSignIn.jsx` (terms, and a name when missing) from `ProtectedRoute` for `sso:` accounts; helpers `lib/sso.js`. **SS3 done** (migration `20260318100000_sso_enforcement.sql`, applied live 2026-11-16; plan §15): `app.mfa_satisfied` (used by every membership helper) is now two-factor AND single sign-on — while `sso_mode = 'required'` only a session whose `amr` names the organisation's **own active provider** (anywhere in the array), or a **break-glass** owner/admin at `aal2`, is a member; **a token with no `amr` (system work acting for a person) is exempt**, so SQL tests and system impersonation need no `amr`, but e2e tests of SSO must pass `amr` in `token(user, claims)`. A company sign-in meets `require_mfa` while `sso_trust_idp_mfa` is on. `app.set_sso_mode` (required needs the caller signed in through the provider now), `app.set_sso_trust_idp_mfa`, `app.set_sso_break_glass` (≤ 3 owners/admins), `sso_sign_ins` (API login, hourly), `sso_changes` (frozen, notice with no actor, emailed; `app.emit_notification` = the 20260303100000 version + `sso_changes` — **the latest definition**), `app.platform_recover_sso` (required → optional), 12-hour session default while required (`app.max_session_hours`), invitations only at verified SSO domains while required, provider/domain kept while not off. API: guard `403 sso_required {providerId, breakGlass}` (before `mfa_required`), `PUT /security/sso/mode|two-factor|break-glass`, `/me` `sso.requiredBy`, `POST /platform/organisations/{id}/recover-sso`, invitations at SSO domains go as a `/sso?domain=` link through the mail provider, never a Supabase password invite. UI `Settings/SsoMode.jsx`, `SsoRequired.jsx` (`qms:sso-required`), operator **Make optional**. **SS4 done** (migration `20260319100000_sso_joining_and_moves.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17; plan §16; pushing the API before it breaks `/me` and the member list): joining on the first company sign-in when `sso_join = 'domain'` (`app.set_sso_joining`: viewer or inspector only, optional department, no seat; `app.join_by_company_sign_in`, `POST /me/sso-join`; owners told), and **moving a password member onto their company account** — `app.user_reference_columns()` classifies **every column that names a user** (membership / moved / history / personal; a `_by` column is history unless listed as moved) and **`supabase/tests/sso_joining_moves.test.sql` fails when a new column naming a user is not classified there** (add it when you add such a column; open work and standing responsibilities are `moved` and need an UPDATE step in `app.sso_do_account_move`). The move re-points the membership (roles, departments, seat, 8D team places follow), then moves open work kind by kind (refusals reported in `skipped`, frozen `sso_account_moves`); by a member manager (`POST /members/{id}/move-to-sso`, matches `GET /members/sso-matches`, dismiss) or by the person with their old password (`POST /me/move-to-sso`, `PasswordVerifier` = Supabase password grant). UI: Who can join in `SsoMode.jsx`, `CompanyAccountCards.jsx` on Start your organisation, `SsoMatches.jsx` + "Company sign-in" mark on Settings → Users. **SSX done except the live check (exit report `docs/sso-exit-report.md`; migration `20260320100000_sso_exit_fixes.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17):** live guardrails 0 violations, advisors nothing new, and **`npm run journey` has a single sign-on step** (`singleSignOn()` in `scripts/journey/journey.cjs`; the stand-in Supabase in `stack.cjs` answers `signInWithSSO` with its own small identity-provider page (`/idp`) that comes back with a token whose `amr` names the provider, and the API's `SupabaseSsoAdmin`, `DnsTxtResolver` and `PasswordVerifier` are swapped for in-memory ones; password tokens now carry `amr` too, as Supabase's do): domain proved with a DNS record, sign-in connected, joining as inspectors, Colin made break-glass, the owner brings her own account across with her old password and requires SSO, Colin's password refused (break-glass at `aal2` checked through the API), Colin moved by the owner, a newcomer joins. The browser found three faults, all fixed: **a member's company account was offered "Join" as well as "Bring your account across"** (`app.sso_joinable_organisations` now leaves out an organisation where a password account with the same address is a member, unless marked as someone else), **`app.my_visible_permissions` ignored the subscription seat check** (`app.has_entitlement`), so seatless members saw cards the server refused, and **the shared confirmation pop-up (`ConfirmationModalProvider`) had no dialog role** (now an `alertdialog` named by its title). The live sign-in against a real identity provider waits for Pro (steps in the exit report).
- **Seats for everyone and member onboarding** (`docs/seats-and-member-onboarding-plan.md`, decisions SM1–SM7 agreed 2026-11-17: managing needs a Controller seat, the server refuses a seatless member, admin rights need no seat; slices SM-A, SM-B, SM-C, SM-X). **SM-A done** (migration `20260321100000_every_feature_priced.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17, section 6 run separately): **every permission is tied to a package** — `features` rows for every module (view = `*.read` and `audit.read`; operational = `audits.perform`, `competence.assess`, `receiving.manage` and the earlier record rights; full = every manage / approve / configure / issue / verify, `ncr.approve_disposition`, `lookups.manage`, `custom_fields.manage`, `workflows.manage`), new modules production, competence, management review, workflows, master data, the planned ones active; package matrix Observer view / Contributor operational (view for control plans, risk, analytics, management review, workflows, master data) / Controller full; integrations Controller only. **Admin rights are never priced**: `app.unpriced_permissions()` = `app.gated_permissions()` + `members.*`, `roles.manage`, `security.*`. **A new permission must be given a feature or listed there**: the migration's last block and `supabase/tests/every_feature_priced.test.sql` fail otherwise. **`app.member_may(org, user, permission)`** answers for someone other than the caller, seat included (role held organisation-wide AND package level reached where enforced); `app.document_signer_candidates` uses it, so only people who may approve are offered as a document's approver (found by the journey: a Contributor was offered and then refused). Grandfathered organisations are unchanged. The journey gives Colin a Controller seat before the document step (`giveColinController`). **SM-B done** (migration `20260322100000_no_seat_no_access.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17; pushing the API before it breaks every organisation route, because the guard calls `app.seat_blocked_organisations()`): **no seat, no access** where packages are enforced — `app.seat_satisfied` is folded into `app.mfa_satisfied`, so every membership helper, RLS rule and function treats a seatless member as not a member, **except** a member holding one of `app.seat_exempt_permissions()` (organisation.manage, members.manage, roles.manage, security.manage, subscription.manage / .seats, billing_details.manage, payment_methods.manage — read-only admin rights do not exempt), who keeps running the account but uses nothing priced. Grandfathered organisations are unchanged. `seat_requests` (kind joined | reminder; written by a deferred trigger at commit when a seatless member becomes active, and by `app.remind_seat_managers(org)` at most once a day, `QM009`; frozen, audited) → notice `seat_requests.created` to holders of `subscription.seats` (emailed; link `/Settings/Users`). `app.my_seat_wait(org)` (waiting, last told, who can give a seat). API: the guard answers **`403 seat_required {organisationId}`** (after `mfa_required`); `/me` leaves such organisations out of `memberships` and lists them in `seats.waitingIn [{id, name, managers, lastToldAt}]`; `POST /me/seat-reminder`. Tests `no_seat_no_access.test.sql`, `api/test/seats.e2e-spec.ts`. **SM-C done** (migration `20260323100000_member_onboarding.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17; pushing the API before it breaks `/me`): **a member's own set-up, separate from the owner's wizard** — `member_onboarding` (one row per membership, written by a trigger when someone joins an organisation that already has members (accepted invitation, company sign-in join); the founder and members from before never get one; `app.my_pending_welcomes()`, `app.set_member_onboarding_step(org, about|notifications|done)`). API `/me` `welcome [{id, name, aboutDone, notificationsDone, waitingForSeat}]`, `POST /me/welcome {organisationId, step}` (works while waiting for a seat). UI: `ProtectedRoute` shows `onboarding/MemberWelcome.jsx` (About you → How we reach you → Ready / Go to Fortress) while a welcome is open, and `onboarding/SeatWaiting.jsx` (who can give a seat, when they were told, **Remind them** once a day, polls `/me` every 30 s, Sign out) inside `WelcomeShell` when the person is waiting for a seat; `App.jsx` shows the waiting screen for the current organisation and refreshes `/me` on `qms:seat-required` (`apiFetch`, `SEAT_REQUIRED_EVENT` in `lib/memberOnboarding.js`). The journey's colleague goes through the welcome after accepting, and the SSO newcomer waits for a seat until the owner gives one on Settings → Users. Tests `member_onboarding.test.sql`, `seats.e2e-spec.ts`, `MemberWelcome.test.jsx`, `App.test.jsx`. **SM-X done** (exit report `docs/seats-and-member-onboarding-exit-report.md`: live guardrails 0 violations, advisors nothing new; the journey's welcome and seat-waiting steps). The seats plan is complete.
- **Edit conflicts** (`docs/edit-conflicts-plan.md`, decisions EC-D1–EC-D6 agreed 2026-11-17; slices EC1–EC4; **EC1 done**, migration `20260324100000_edit_conflicts.sql`, applied live 2026-11-17): a save may say which version of a record it was based on, and the database refuses it when the record changed since. The version is `updated_at` to the microsecond as text (`app.row_version`); the API puts `{ "<table>:<id>": "<version>" }` in the transaction setting `app.expected_versions`; **`app.touch_updated()` (replaced) checks it inside the update** (`QM031` on a different version; a match is used up, so later updates of the same row in that request pass) — the same for RLS updates and SECURITY DEFINER functions; `integration_connections` and `workflow_rules` get the check alone (`app.check_edit_version`). No setting = last write wins, as before (imports, worker, scripts). `app.edit_conflict_details(table, id, since)` (members only, else `P0002`) gives the current version, who changed it last and every field changed since, from `audit.events`. **A function `touch_updated` calls must be executable by everyone who updates edited tables** (`authenticated`, `qms_api`, `service_role`). Test `supabase/tests/edit_conflicts.test.sql` (a broken check is caught). **EC2 done** (API, no migration): **`@Versioned('<table>')` on a record's GET and PATCH/PUT** (`api/src/common/versioned.ts`, global `VersionInterceptor`) adds **`rowVersion`** (not `version`: audits, workflow rules and part revisions already use that name; **never an `ETag`**: an answer holds child rows that change without moving the version, so the browser would get a 304 and show a stale copy) to an answer that is the route's `:id` record; a save sends `rowVersion` in the body (removed before the strict body check; a malformed one is ignored) or `If-Match`; the interceptor puts it in `RequestContext.expectedVersions` and `DatabaseService.asUser` passes it as `app.expected_versions`; `QM031` → **`409 edit_conflict`** with `details { version, changedAt, changedBy, fields (API names), people }` and the message "<name> changed this since you opened it".
